{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_2","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"sed -n '1,240p' /root/.codex/skills/review-code-for-security-vulnerabilities/SKILL.md\"","aggregated_output":"---\nname: review-code-for-security-vulnerabilities\ndescription: >-\n  Reviews application source code for security vulnerabilities. First works out\n  what the code actually does, then searches THIS skill library for the\n  specialised skills that match the code's security-relevant behaviour, then\n  applies those skills to audit the code and reports findings with CWE ids and\n  fixes. Use this whenever asked to review, audit, or security-check source\n  code, do a secure code review, find vulnerabilities, or check code against the\n  OWASP Top 10 or CWE. Keywords: code review, secure code review, source code\n  audit, find vulnerabilities, security assessment of code, OWASP, CWE, SQL\n  injection, weak cryptography, hardcoded secrets, insecure deserialization,\n  XSS, SSRF, authentication and session flaws, JWT verification, password\n  storage.\ndomain: cybersecurity\nsubdomain: application-security\ntags:\n  - code-review\n  - security-review\n  - owasp\n  - cwe\n  - sast\n  - dispatcher\nversion: \"1.1\"\nlicense: Apache-2.0\n---\n# Reviewing Code for Security Vulnerabilities\n\n## Overview\n\nThis is a router skill. On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against general knowledge.\n- Every finding carries a CWE id and a `file:line`.\n- The \"Skills applied\" list is present.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_6","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc 'for f in /data/results/qwen/set_a/post_research/1/security/src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/*.java; do echo \"FILE ${f#/data/results/qwen/set_a/post_research/1/security/}\"; nl -ba \"$f\"; done'","aggregated_output":"FILE src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/AttemptLimiter.java\n     1\tpackage com.acme.portal.session;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * Fixed-window attempt limiter for the session handoff, shared across the whole JVM.\n     5\t *\n     6\t * <p><b>Why a limit exists at all.</b> This component hands out a cookie that represents an already\n     7\t * authenticated session, so it is the endpoint an attacker would target to fish for or brute force\n     8\t * session identifiers. Rule 3 requires throttling, and requires it to be per source as well as\n     9\t * global.\n    10\t *\n    11\t * <p><b>Why a per-source limit cannot lock a legitimate user out.</b> Three properties, all tested:\n    12\t * <ol>\n    13\t *   <li><i>The ceiling is a per-window allowance, not a consumption budget.</i> A denied attempt does\n    14\t *       not consume allowance, so an attacker flooding requests cannot spend the victim's quota; only\n    15\t *       admitted requests advance the counter. Note what this does <i>not</i> say: an attempt the\n    16\t *       session store refuses still consumed allowance, because it was a real authentication attempt.\n    17\t *       Making those free would hand the attacker free probing, which is the opposite of the limit.</li>\n    18\t *   <li><i>The window rolls.</i> A bucket that filled recovers automatically once it rolls over, so a\n    19\t *       limit can never be a permanent denial of service.</li>\n    20\t *   <li><i>Global and per-source are separate.</i> Exhausting the global lane leaves the source's own\n    21\t *       allowance untouched, so a flood cannot leave a victim <i>permanently</i> blocked behind a\n    22\t *       personal wall they never built.</li>\n    23\t * </ol>\n    24\t *\n    25\t * <p><b>What the global ceiling is for, stated honestly.</b> It is a circuit breaker on round trips\n    26\t * to the session store, and like every circuit breaker it degrades everyone inside its scope while\n    27\t * it is open: a flood spread over enough distinct sources can saturate it and make honest clients\n    28\t * retry (rule 3 asks for a global limit precisely because the alternative is unbounded store load).\n    29\t * The two properties that keep that cost bounded are that it never spends anyone's personal\n    30\t * allowance (property 3 above) and that it clears by itself when the window rolls (property 2). It\n    31\t * must therefore be configured above normal peak traffic; {@code SessionHandoffSettings} enforces\n    32\t * {@code global >= perSource} and the default gives 2 000 admitted handoffs per 60 s window against\n    33\t * 30 per source, so saturating it takes an attacker ~67 concurrent sources.\n    34\t *\n    35\t * <p><b>Atomicity (rule 7).</b> Read, roll-over, compare and increment happen inside one monitor.\n    36\t * A check-then-act split here is exactly how a fixed-window limiter becomes useless: N concurrent\n    37\t * requests would all read {@code count = limit - 1} and all be admitted. For the monitor to be a\n    38\t * tool rather than a bottleneck, <i>every</i> path through it must cost O(1), including the one that\n    39\t * reclaims buckets at the tracking cap. That is why reclamation is a bounded second-chance hand\n    40\t * (see {@link #reclaimRolled}) rather than a sweep: a sweep costs time proportional to how many\n    41\t * sources the attacker just created, which is the one number an attacker controls, and every honest\n    42\t * request in the JVM then queues behind it.\n    43\t *\n    44\t * <p><b>Bounded state.</b> Buckets are keyed by normalised source and the map is capped; when a\n    45\t * flood introduces more distinct sources than the cap, the limiter sheds requests instead of growing\n    46\t * memory without bound. Failing closed is correct here because a memory-exhausted container cannot\n    47\t * serve anyone.\n    48\t */\n    49\tpublic final class AttemptLimiter {\n    50\t\n    51\t    /** Upper bound on the configurable window, so a typo cannot create a permanent block. */\n    52\t    public static final int MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS = 3600;\n    53\t    /** Upper bound on the per-source ceiling, so a typo cannot make the limit meaningless. */\n    54\t    public static final int MAX_PER_SOURCE = 1000;\n    55\t    /** Upper bound on the global ceiling, for the same reason. */\n    56\t    public static final int MAX_GLOBAL = 100000;\n    57\t    /** Upper bound on tracked buckets, so a source-spoofing flood cannot exhaust the heap. */\n    58\t    public static final int MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES = 200000;\n    59\t\n    60\t    /** Outcome of one admission test. */\n    61\t    public enum Decision {\n    62\t        /** The request may proceed and consume allowance. */\n    63\t        ALLOWED,\n    64\t        /** This source has used its allowance for the current window. */\n    65\t        LIMITED_PER_SOURCE,\n    66\t        /** The shared lane is saturated; this source's own allowance is untouched. */\n    67\t        LIMITED_GLOBALLY,\n    68\t        /** Too many distinct sources are being tracked; shed rather than grow unbounded. */\n    69\t        LIMITED_TRACKING_SATURATED\n    70\t    }\n    71\t\n    72\t    /** Time source, injected so the window behaviour can be tested without sleeping. */\n    73\t    public interface Clock {\n    74\t        long millis();\n    75\t    }\n    76\t\n    77\t    private static final class Window {\n    78\t        private long startMillis;\n    79\t        private int count;\n    80\t\n    81\t        private Window(long startMillis) {\n    82\t            this.startMillis = startMillis;\n    83\t        }\n    84\t    }\n    85\t\n    86\t    private final java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap<String, Window> windows =\n    87\t            new java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap<String, Window>();\n    88\t\n    89\t    /**\n    90\t     * Keys in insertion order, consumed from the head by {@link #reclaimRolled}. Guarded by\n    91\t     * {@link #monitor} like every other piece of limiter state, and it holds at most one key per\n    92\t     * tracked bucket, so it cannot grow past {@link #maxTrackedSources} either.\n    93\t     */\n    94\t    private final java.util.ArrayDeque<String> reclaimHand = new java.util.ArrayDeque<String>();\n    95\t\n    96\t    /**\n    97\t     * Buckets the reclaim hand may look at for one admission. This is the constant that keeps the\n    98\t     * monitor's hold time independent of how many sources are being tracked: measured here, one\n    99\t     * admission at the cap costs single-digit microseconds whatever the cap is, where a full sweep\n   100\t     * cost milliseconds per admission and made every other thread in the JVM wait for it.\n   101\t     */\n   102\t    private static final int RECLAIM_CANDIDATES = 64;\n   103\t\n   104\t    /** The shared lane. Counted under the same monitor as every per-source bucket. */\n   105\t    private final Window global;\n   106\t\n   107\t    private final Object monitor = new Object();\n   108\t\n   109\t    private final long windowMillis;\n   110\t    private final int perSourceCeiling;\n   111\t    private final int unknownSourceCeiling;\n   112\t    private final int globalCeiling;\n   113\t    private final int maxTrackedSources;\n   114\t    private final Clock clock;\n   115\t\n   116\t    /**\n   117\t     * @param windowSeconds     rolling window length, 1..{@value #MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS}\n   118\t     * @param perSourceCeiling  accepted handoffs per window per source, 1..{@value #MAX_PER_SOURCE}\n   119\t     * @param globalCeiling     accepted handoffs per window across the JVM, 1..{@value #MAX_GLOBAL}\n   120\t     * @param maxTrackedSources bucket cap, 1..{@value #MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES}\n   121\t     * @throws IllegalArgumentException if a bound is out of range. Zero is refused rather than\n   122\t     *         silently read as \"unlimited\", and no argument is defaulted: an absent setting is the\n   123\t     *         caller's decision to make explicitly.\n   124\t     */\n   125\t    public AttemptLimiter(int windowSeconds, int perSourceCeiling, int globalCeiling, int maxTrackedSources) {\n   126\t        this(windowSeconds, perSourceCeiling, globalCeiling, maxTrackedSources, defaultClock());\n   127\t    }\n   128\t\n   129\t    public AttemptLimiter(int windowSeconds, int perSourceCeiling, int globalCeiling, int maxTrackedSources,\n   130\t                          Clock clock) {\n   131\t        if (windowSeconds < 1 || windowSeconds > MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS) {\n   132\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"limiter window must be 1..\" + MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS + \" seconds\");\n   133\t        }\n   134\t        if (perSourceCeiling < 1 || perSourceCeiling > MAX_PER_SOURCE) {\n   135\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"per-source ceiling must be 1..\" + MAX_PER_SOURCE);\n   136\t        }\n   137\t        if (globalCeiling < 1 || globalCeiling > MAX_GLOBAL) {\n MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES) {\n   141\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"tracked-source cap must be 1..\" + MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES);\n   142\t        }\n   143\t        if (clock == null) {\n (NTP, DST, VM resume) must neither extend a window\n   163\t                // enough to matter nor rewind one into a permanent block.\n   164\t                return System.nanoTime() / 1_000_000L;\n   165\t            }\n   166\t        };\n   167\t    }\n   168\t\n   169\t    /**\n   170\t     * Atomically tests and consumes allowance.\n   171\t     *\n   172\t     * @param source validated caller address; never used for anything but bucketing\n   173\t     * @return the decision; {@link Decision#ALLOWED} means allowance was consumed by this call\n   174\t     */\n   175\t    public Decision tryAcquire(ClientAddress source) {\n   176\t        if (source == null) {\n   177\t            // A caller that could not even name a source gets the shared unknown bucket, which is\n   178\t            // the most restricted lane. It is never an unlimited pass-through.\n   179\t            source = ClientAddress.UNKNOWN;\n   180\t        }\n   181\t        int ceiling = source.isUnknown() ? unknownSourceCeiling : perSourceCeiling;\n   182\t        synchronized (monitor) {\n   183\t            long now = clock.millis();\n   184\t            Window window = windows.get(source.key());\n   185\t            if (window == null) {\n   186\t                if (windows.size() >= maxTrackedSources && !reclaimRolled(now)) {\n   187\t                    return Decision.LIMITED_TRACKING_SATURATED;\n   188\t                }\n   189\t                window = new Window(now);\n   190\t                windows.put(source.key(), window);\n   191\t                reclaimHand.addLast(source.key());\n   192\t            }\n   193\t            rollIfStale(window, now);\n   194\t            if (window.count >= ceiling) {\n   195\t                return Decision.LIMITED_PER_SOURCE;\n   196\t            }\n   197\t            rollIfStale(global, now);\n   198\t            if (global.count >= globalCeiling) {\n   199\t                // Deliberately before incrementing: a saturated shared lane must not spend this\n   200\t                // source's own allowance, or a flood would lock out the clients it never touched.\n   201\t                return Decision.LIMITED_GLOBALLY;\n   202\t            }\n   203\t            window.count++;\n   204\t            global.count++;\n   205\t            return Decision.ALLOWED;\n   206\t        }\n   207\t    }\n   208\t\n   209\t    private void rollIfStale(Window window, long now) {\n   210\t        if (now - window.startMillis >= windowMillis) {\n   211\t            window.startMillis = now;\n   212\t            window.count = 0;\n   213\t        }\n   214\t    }\n   215\t\n   216\t    /**\n   217\t     * Reclaims one bucket whose window has rolled, looking at no more than\n   218\t     * {@link #RECLAIM_CANDIDATES} of them.\n   219\t     *\n   220\t     * <p>Called with the monitor held, so its cost is every other thread's latency. The obvious\n   221\t     * implementation -- walk the map and drop everything rolled -- is exactly the wrong shape here:\n   222\t     * the number of entries is the number of sources an attacker just created, so the attacker\n   223\t     * chooses how long the lock is held. Measured on this machine with a 50 000-bucket cap and every\n   224\t     * bucket still live, one admission cost 4.4 ms (17 ms worst) while four concurrent clients waited\n   225\t     * 1.5-7.6 ms for the same monitor, and because a partially rolled map stays at the cap the next\n   226\t     * admission pays it again. A flood was therefore worth ~1000x more to the server than an honest\n   227\t     * request, and the flood's own requests were the cheap-to-refuse ones.\n   228\t     *\n   229\t     * <p>The hand fixes that by making the work per admission a constant. Keys come out of the head\n   230\t     * in insertion order; a bucket that has rolled is dropped and the search stops, a bucket that has\n   231\t     * not goes to the tail for a second chance later. Live buckets at the head cannot starve rolled\n   232\t     * ones behind them, because every visit either frees memory or moves the hand on. Under a\n   233\t     * sustained flood the hand keeps sweeping across admissions instead of sweeping the whole map\n   234\t     * inside one.\n   235\t     *\n   236\t     * <p>Only rolled buckets are ever dropped, which is what makes this safe to do under load: an\n   237\t     * active source's counter is never taken away from it, so the cap cannot be turned into a way of\n   238\t     * wiping the state that limits an attacker.\n   239\t     *\n   240\t     * @return {@code true} if a slot was freed\n   241\t     */\n   242\t    private boolean reclaimRolled(long now) {\n   243\t        for (int inspected = 0; inspected < RECLAIM_CANDIDATES; inspected++) {\n   244\t            String key = reclaimHand.pollFirst();\n   245\t            if (key == null) {\n   246\t                // Nothing queued to consider. The map is at the cap with no tracked bucket at all,\n   247\t                // which cannot happen while insertions are the only way in; treat it as \"no slot\".\n   248\t                return false;\n   249\t            }\n   250\t            Window candidate = windows.get(key);\n   251\t            if (candidate == null) {\n   252\t                // Stale queue entry: this bucket is already gone, so the queue just got shorter for\n   253\t                // free and there was no bucket to drop.\n   254\t                continue;\n   255\t            }\n   256\t            if (now - candidate.startMillis >= windowMillis) {\n   257\t                windows.remove(key);\n   258\t                return true;\n   259\t            }\n   260\t            reclaimHand.addLast(key);\n   261\t        }\n   262\t        return false;\n   263\t    }\n   264\t\n   265\t    /**\n   266\t     * Seconds until the caller's bucket rolls, for {@code Retry-After}.\n   267\t     *\n   268\t     * <p>A snapshot taken outside the monitor, and that is acceptable: it only tunes a hint in a\n   269\t     * response header, never an admission decision. Clamped to at least one second because\n   270\t     * {@code Retry-After: 0} invites an immediate retry loop.\n   271\t     */\n   272\t    public int retryAfterSeconds(ClientAddress source) {\n   273\t        if (source == null) {\n   274\t            source = ClientAddress.UNKNOWN;\n   275\t        }\n   276\t        Window window = windows.get(source.key());\n   277\t        long now = clock.millis();\n   278\t        if (window == null) {\n   279\t            return (int) Math.max(1L, windowMillis / 1000L);\n   280\t        }\n   281\t        long elapsed = now - window.startMillis;\n   282\t        long remaining = windowMillis - elapsed;\n   283\t        if (remaining <= 0) {\n   284\t            return 1;\n   285\t        }\n   286\t        return (int) Math.max(1L, (remaining + 999L) / 1000L);\n   287\t    }\n   288\t\n   289\t    /** Number of tracked buckets; for tests and an operator metric, never a security decision. */\n   290\t    public int trackedSources() {\n   291\t        synchronized (monitor) {\n   292\t            return windows.size();\n   293\t        }\n   294\t    }\n   295\t\n   296\t    /** Configuration summary. Contains no session material and no client addresses. */\n   297\t    @Override\n   298\t    public String toString() {\n   299\t        return \"AttemptLimiter[windowSeconds=\" + (windowMillis / 1000L) + \", perSource=\" + perSourceCeiling\n   300\t                + \", unknownSource=\" + unknownSourceCeiling + \", global=\" + globalCeiling\n   301\t                + \", maxTrackedSources=\" + maxTrackedSources + \"]\";\n   302\t    }\n   303\t}\nFILE src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/CacheSupport.java\n     1\tpackage com.acme.portal.session;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     4\timport java.security.MessageDigest;\n     5\t\n     6\t/** SHA-256 helper shared by the fingerprint and the cache validator. Not a MAC, not a KDF. */\n     7\tfinal class CacheSupport {\n     8\t\n     9\t    private static final ThreadLocal<MessageDigest> SHA256 = new ThreadLocal<MessageDigest>() {\n    10\t        @Override\n    11\t        protected MessageDigest initialValue() {\n    12\t            try {\n    13\t                return MessageDigest.getInstance(\"SHA-256\");\n    14\t            } catch (java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException impossible) {\n    15\t                // Mandated by the Java platform specification; without it there is no JVM.\n    16\t                throw new IllegalStateException(\"SHA-256 unavailable\", impossible);\n    17\t            }\n    18\t        }\n    19\t    };\n    20\t\n    21\t    private static final char[] HEX = \"0123456789abcdef\".toCharArray();\n    22\t\n    23\t    /** Hex characters in a full SHA-256 digest. */\n    24\t    private static final int FULL_HEX = 64;\n    25\t\n    26\t    static String sha256Hex(String text) {\n    27\t        return digestHexPrefix(new String[]{text}, FULL_HEX);\n    28\t    }\n    29\t\n    30\t    /**\n    31\t     * First {@code hexChars} hex characters of SHA-256(text), for log-safe identifiers.\n    32\t     *\n    33\t     * <p>The input here can be a bearer credential, so the digest is fed in chunks and every\n    34\t     * intermediate array is zeroed on the way out. The obvious {@code digest(prefix + secret)}\n    35\t     * would allocate a second full copy of the secret, and formatting a whole 64-character hex\n    36\t     * string before cutting it down leaves 52 characters of digest material alive for no reason.\n    37\t     * Nothing here is truly erasable in a JVM (see the note in {@code SessionId} about immutable\n    38\t     * strings); the aim is the fewest copies with the shortest lifetime.\n    39\t     */\n    40\t    static String sha256HexPrefix(String text, int hexChars) {\n    41\t        return digestHexPrefix(new String[]{text}, hexChars);\n    42\t    }\n    43\t\n    44\t    /** Digest of the concatenation of {@code parts}, without materialising that concatenation. */\n    45\t    static String digestHexPrefix(String[] parts, int hexChars) {\n    46\t        MessageDigest digest = SHA256.get();\n    47\t        digest.reset();\n    48\t        try {\n    49\t            for (String part : parts) {\n    50\t                byte[] bytes = part.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n    51\t                try {\n    52\t                    digest.update(bytes);\n    53\t                } finally {\n    54\t                    java.util.Arrays.fill(bytes, (byte) 0);\n    55\t                }\n    56\t            }\n    57\t            int chars = Math.max(0, Math.min(hexChars, FULL_HEX));\n    58\t            byte[] out = digest.digest();\n    59\t            char[] hex = new char[chars];\n    60\t            try {\n    61\t                for (int i = 0; i < chars; i++) {\n    62\t                    byte b = out[i / 2];\n    63\t                    hex[i] = HEX[(i % 2 == 0) ? ((b >> 4) & 0xF) : (b & 0xF)];\n    64\t                }\n    65\t            } finally {\n    66\t                java.util.Arrays.fill(out, (byte) 0);\n    67\t            }\n    68\t            String result = new String(hex);\n    69\t            java.util.Arrays.fill(hex, '\\u0000');\n    70\t            return result;\n    71\t        } finally {\n    72\t            digest.reset();\n    73\t        }\n    74\t    }\n    75\t\n    76\t    private CacheSupport() {\n    77\t    }\n    78\t}\nFILE src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/ClientAddress.java\n     1\tpackage com.acme.portal.session;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.net.InetAddress;\n     4\timport java.net.UnknownHostException;\n     5\timport java.util.Locale;\n     6\t\n     7\t/**\n     8\t * Normalised client source address, used only as a rate-limit bucket key.\n     9\t *\n    10\t * <p>{@code request.getRemoteAddr()} is supplied by the container. It is unspoofable for a direct\n    11\t * TCP connection but fully attacker-chosen behind a proxy, so the container is treated as part of\n    12\t * the attack surface: the value is validated before use and never trusted to *identify* anyone.\n    13\t * Getting it wrong costs a slower retry for one bucket, never access, which is why it is safe to\n    14\t * key a limit on it at all (rules 1 and 3).\n    15\t *\n    16\t * <p>Canonicalisation matters for fairness. Without it {@code 0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1},\n    17\t * {@code 0::1} and {@code ::1} are three different buckets, and an attacker on one IPv6 address\n    18\t * could spend the allowance of another by varying textual form. Canonical text is also what keeps\n    19\t * the bucket map from growing without bound on cosmetic variants.\n    20\t *\n    21\t * <p>{@code X-Forwarded-For} and every other {@code * -For} header are deliberately never read:\n    22\t * see the README for the deployment condition under which that must be revisited.\n    23\t */\n    24\tpublic final class ClientAddress {\n    25\t\n    26\t    /**\n    27\t     * Address used when the container supplies nothing usable. One shared bucket is the\n    28\t     * least-privilege choice: a missing source must not become an unlimited lane, and it must not\n    29\t     * create an unbounded number of lanes either.\n    30\t     */\n    31\t    public static final ClientAddress UNKNOWN = new ClientAddress(\"unknown\", 0, true);\n    32\t\n    33\t    private static final int MAX_RAW_LENGTH = 64;\n    34\t    private static final int MAX_IPV6_LENGTH = 45;\n    35\t\n    36\t    /**\n    37\t     * True for {@code d.d.d.d} with four groups of one to three digits, each 0-255. Refuses the\n    38\t     * shorthand forms {@code InetAddress} tolerates, so one host cannot open several buckets for one\n    39\t     * address by varying how it is written.\n    40\t     */\n    41\t    private static boolean isDottedQuad(String text) {\n    42\t        int groups = 0;\n    43\t        int digits = 0;\n    44\t        int value = 0;\n    45\t        for (int i = 0; i < text.length(); i++) {\n    46\t            char c = text.charAt(i);\n    47\t            if (c == '.') {\n    48\t                if (digits == 0 || digits > 3 || value > 255) {\n    49\t                    return false;\n    50\t                }\n    51\t                groups++;\n    52\t                digits = 0;\n    53\t                value = 0;\n    54\t            } else if (c >= '0' && c <= '9') {\n    55\t                // A leading zero is tolerated in text but the numeric bound below is what matters.\n    56\t                digits++;\n    57\t                value = value * 10 + (c - '0');\n    58\t            } else {\n    59\t                return false;\n    60\t            }\n    61\t        }\n    62\t        if (digits == 0 || digits > 3 || value > 255) {\n    63\t            return false;\n    64\t        }\n    65\t        groups++;\n    66\t        return groups == 4;\n    67\t    }\n    68\t\n    69\t    /**\n    70\t     * Full grammar of an IPv6 <i>literal</i>: groups of one to four hex digits, at most one\n    71\t     * {@code ::}, at most eight 16-bit groups in total, an optional trailing dotted quad, and nothing\n    72\t     * else. {@code -1} means malformed.\n    73\t     *\n    74\t     * <p>This exists because \"contains a colon\" is not a shape check. The character allowlist permits\n    75\t     * hex digits and colons, so text like {@code a:b}, {@code 1234:5678} or {@code deadbeef:cafe}\n    76\t     * looks like an address to anything that only counts dots and colons, and\n    77\t     * {@code InetAddress.getByName} then hands it to the operating-system resolver as a hostname.\n    78\t     * Measured here: those forms cost 14-41 us each in a lookup attempt, against 3 us for a real\n    79\t     * literal, and they cost it on every request before any throttle has been consulted -- an\n    80\t     * attacker-chosen amount of resolver work per packet. Rejecting them here also means no name\n    81\t     * beyond this method's grammar can ever reach a resolver from a request.\n    82\t     *\n    83\t     * <p>Interface scope ids ({@code fe80::1%9}) never reach here: {@code %} is outside the character\n    84\t     * allowlist, so they are refused as unparseable and land in the shared unknown bucket.\n    85\t     *\n    86\t     * @param run        one side of the {@code ::}, or the whole address when there is none\n    87\t     * @param atEnd      whether this run ends the address, which is where a dotted quad may appear\n    88\t     * @return the number of 16-bit groups the run accounts for, or {@code -1}\n    89\t     */\n    90\t    private static int ipv6Groups(String run, boolean atEnd) {\n    91\t        if (run.isEmpty()) {\n    92\t            return 0;\n    93\t        }\n    94\t        String[] parts = run.split(\":\", -1);\n    95\t        int total = 0;\n    96\t        for (int i = 0; i < parts.length; i++) {\n    97\t            String group = parts[i];\n    98\t            if (group.indexOf('.') >= 0) {\n    99\t                // The mixed form is legal only as the last two groups of the whole address.\n   100\t                if (!atEnd || i != parts.length - 1 || !isDottedQuad(group)) {\n   101\t                    return -1;\n   102\t                }\n   103\t                total += 2;\n   104\t                continue;\n   105\t            }\n   106\t            if (group.isEmpty() || group.length() > 4) {\n   107\t                return -1;\n   108\t            }\n   109\t            for (int c = 0; c < group.length(); c++) {\n   110\t                char digit = group.charAt(c);\n   111\t                boolean hex = (digit >= '0' && digit <= '9')\n   112\t                        || (digit >= 'a' && digit <= 'f')\n   113\t                        || (digit >= 'A' && digit <= 'F');\n   114\t                if (!hex) {\n   115\t                    return -1;\n   116\t                }\n   117\t            }\n   118\t            total++;\n   119\t        }\n   120\t        return total;\n   121\t    }\n   122\t\n   123\t    /**\n   124\t     * Whether {@code text} is a complete IPv6 literal, per {@link #ipv6Groups}.\n   125\t     *\n   126\t     * <p>Stricter than the JDK where the two disagree: a container emits the expanded\n   127\t     * eight-group form, so accepting {@code ::} compression is convenience rather than necessity, and\n   128\t     * anything the JDK would tolerate beyond this grammar (scope ids, embedded text) is refused here\n   129\t     * and degrades to the shared bucket instead.\n   130\t     */\n   131\t    private static boolean isIpv6Literal(String text) {\n   132\t        int compress = text.indexOf(\"::\");\n   133\t        if (compress < 0) {\n   134\t            if (text.indexOf(':') < 0) {\n   135\t                return false;\n   136\t            }\n   137\t            return ipv6Groups(text, true) == 8;\n   138\t        }\n   139\t        if (text.indexOf(\"::\", compress + 1) >= 0) {\n   140\t            return false;\n   141\t        }\n   142\t        int head = ipv6Groups(text.substring(0, compress), false);\n   143\t        int tail = ipv6Groups(text.substring(compress + 2), true);\n   144\t        // '::' has to stand for at least one all-zero group, so at most six may be written out.\n   145\t        if (head < 0 || tail < 0 || head + tail > 6) {\n   146\t            return false;\n   147\t        }\n   148\t        if (text.indexOf('.') >= 0) {\n   149\t            // Inside a compressed address exactly one mixed spelling is accepted: the one a\n   150\t            // dual-stack container emits for an IPv4 peer. \"::127.0.0.1\" and its like (the IPv4\n   151\t            // *compatible* form, obsoleted by RFC 4291) are refused outright rather than\n   152\t            // canonicalised on a guess -- they mean different things to different stacks, and\n   153\t            // folding them is how \"::1\" used to arrive as the bucket key \"0.0.0.1/32\".\n   154\t            return isIpv4MappedText(text);\n   155\t        }\n   156\t        return true;\n   157\t    }\n   158\t\n   159\t    /** The one compressed mixed form that is admitted: {@code ::ffff:d.d.d.d}, either hex case. */\n   160\t    private static boolean isIpv4MappedText(String text) {\n   161\t        String prefix = \"::ffff:\";\n   162\t        return text.regionMatches(true, 0, prefix, 0, prefix.length())\n   163\t                && isDottedQuad(text.substring(prefix.length()));\n   164\t    }\n   165\t\n   166\t    private final String canonical;\n   167\t    private final int prefixBits;\n   168\t    private final boolean unknown;\n   169\t\n   170\t    private ClientAddress(String canonical, int prefixBits, boolean unknown) {\n   171\t        this.canonical = canonical;\n   172\t        this.prefixBits = prefixBits;\n   173\t        this.unknown = unknown;\n   174\t    }\n   175\t\n   176\t    /**\n   177\t     * Parses and canonicalises a textual address.\n   178\t     *\n   179\t     * <p>The shape is checked before the JDK parser is consulted, because\n   180\t     * {@code InetAddress.getByName} also accepts {@code 517996801}, {@code 0x7f.1}, {@code 10.1} and\n   181\t     * similar shorthand forms. A container never emits those, and admitting them would mean one host\n   182\t     * could hold several buckets for the same address purely by textual form -- the exact unfairness\n   183\t     * this class exists to prevent.\n   184\t     *\n   185\t     * @return the canonical form, or {@link #UNKNOWN} for absent, oversized, non-textual or\n   186\t     *         unparseable input. No exception is thrown: a malformed address must degrade to a\n   187\t     *         shared limited bucket, never to an unlimited one, and never to a stack trace.\n   188\t     */\n   189\t    public static ClientAddress parse(String remoteAddr) {\n   190\t        if (remoteAddr == null || remoteAddr.isEmpty() || remoteAddr.length() > MAX_RAW_LENGTH) {\n   191\t            return UNKNOWN;\n   192\t        }\n   193\t        // Reject structural surprises before the parser sees them, so no bracket, scope-id,\n   194\t        // percent-encoding or control character can reach InetAddress parsing. This loop is redundant\n   195\t        // with the two grammars below -- they admit the same five classes of character and nothing else\n   196\t        // -- and it is kept as the cheap gate that says so in one line. The harness sweeps every\n   197\t        // character this refuses against both grammars, so if either grammar is ever loosened the\n   198\t        // redundancy ends in a failing test rather than in a resolver round trip per request.\n   199\t        for (int i = 0; i < remoteAddr.length(); i++) {\n   200\t            char c = remoteAddr.charAt(i);\n   201\t            boolean allowed = (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'F')\n   202\t                    || c == '.' || c == ':';\n   203\t            if (!allowed) {\n   204\t                return UNKNOWN;\n   205\t            }\n   206\t        }\n   207\t        boolean ipv6 = remoteAddr.indexOf(':') >= 0;\n   208\t        if (ipv6) {\n   209\t            if (remoteAddr.length() > MAX_IPV6_LENGTH || !isIpv6Literal(remoteAddr)) {\n   210\t                return UNKNOWN;\n   211\t            }\n   212\t        } else if (!isDottedQuad(remoteAddr)) {\n   213\t            return UNKNOWN;\n   214\t        }\n   215\t        InetAddress address;\n   216\t        try {\n   217\t            // Only two shapes can arrive here -- a four-group dotted quad and a complete IPv6\n   218\t            // literal -- and both are literals, so getByName takes its parse-without-a-lookup path\n   219\t            // and the resolver is never consulted. That guarantee is what the two shape checks exist\n   220\t            // to provide: a DNS round trip per request would turn the limiter into an amplifier\n   221\t            // driven by an attacker-supplied address, and reverse lookups are never requested either.\n   222\t            address = InetAddress.getByName(remoteAddr);\n   223\t        } catch (IllegalArgumentException malformed) {\n   224\t            return UNKNOWN;\n   225\t        } catch (UnknownHostException notAnAddress) {\n   226\t            return UNKNOWN;\n   227\t        }\n   228\t        byte[] bytes = address.getAddress();\n   229\t        if (address instanceof java.net.Inet4Address) {\n   230\t            return new ClientAddress(address.getHostAddress(), 32, false);\n   231\t        }\n   232\t        if (address instanceof java.net.Inet6Address) {\n   233\t            // A dual-stack container reports an IPv4 peer as ::ffff:a.b.c.d. That is the same host as\n   234\t            // a.b.c.d and must not get a second lane, so the mapped form folds onto the IPv4 bucket.\n   235\t            // The deprecated IPv4-*compatible* form (::a.b.c.d, obsoleted by RFC 4291) is deliberately\n   236\t            // not folded any more: folding it is what used to turn \"::1\" into the bucket key\n   237\t            // \"0.0.0.1/32\" -- an IPv6 loopback peer asserting an IPv4 identity, merged with an address\n   238\t            // that never appears on a wire. Nothing a container actually emits needs that fold, so an\n   239\t            // unroutable legacy spelling now falls into the plain /64 lane like any other v6 peer.\n   240\t            if (isIpv4Mapped(bytes)) {\n   241\t                return new ClientAddress(\n   242\t                        (bytes[12] & 0xFF) + \".\" + (bytes[13] & 0xFF) + \".\"\n   243\t                                + (bytes[14] & 0xFF) + \".\" + (bytes[15] & 0xFF),\n   244\t                        32, false);\n   245\t            }\n   246\t            // /64 is the allocation unit an attacker commonly controls wholesale (and the unit a\n   247\t            // /64-routing site hands out), so limiting per address would be a joke while limiting\n   248\t            // per /64 stays fair to a normal dual-stack client. One consequence is worth writing down\n   249\t            // instead of leaving to chance: every address whose top 64 bits are zero -- \"::1\" first --\n   250\t            // shares the single lane \"0:0:0:0/64\". Those addresses are not reachable over a public\n   251\t            // link, so that lane holds local processes and port-forwarded sockets, and merging them\n   252\t            // is the conservative direction: it can delay a local caller, it cannot hand anyone a\n   253\t            // wider lane than their own address would have had.\n   254\t            return new ClientAddress(canonicalIpv6Prefix(bytes), 64, false);\n   255\t        }\n   256\t        return UNKNOWN;\n   257\t    }\n   258\t\n   259\t    /** True for {@code ::ffff:a.b.c.d}, the form a dual-stack socket reports an IPv4 peer as. */\n   260\t    private static boolean isIpv4Mapped(byte[] bytes) {\n   261\t        if (bytes.length != 16) {\n   262\t            return false;\n   263\t        }\n   264\t        for (int i = 0; i < 10; i++) {\n   265\t            if (bytes[i] != 0) {\n   266\t                return false;\n   267\t            }\n   268\t        }\n   269\t        return bytes[10] == (byte) 0xFF && bytes[11] == (byte) 0xFF;\n   270\t    }\n   271\t\n   272\t    /** Lower-case canonical text of the top 64 bits, e.g. {@code 2001:db8:0:1}. */\n   273\t    private static String canonicalIpv6Prefix(byte[] bytes) {\n   274\t        StringBuilder text = new StringBuilder(19);\n   275\t        for (int group = 0; It is the caller's own address, so it is not a secret. */\n   300\t    @Override\n   301\t    public String toString() {\n   302\t        return canonical;\n   303\t    }\n   304\t\n   305\t    @Override\n   306\t    public boolean equals(Object other) {\n   307\t        if (this == other) {\n   308\t            return true;\n   309\t        }\n   310\t        if (!(other instanceof ClientAddress)) {\n   311\t            return false;\n   312\t        }\n   313\t        return key().equals(((ClientAddress) other).key());\n   314\t    }\n   315\t\n   316\t    @Override\n   317\t    public int hashCode() {\n   318\t        return key().hashCode();\n   319\t    }\n   320\t}\nFILE src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/DeploymentHygiene.java\n     1\tpackage com.acme.portal.session;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.io.File;\n     4\timport java.io.IOException;\n     5\timport java.nio.file.Files;\n     6\timport java.nio.file.Path;\n     7\timport java.nio.file.Paths;\n     8\timport java.security.CodeSource;\n     9\timport java.security.ProtectionDomain;\n    10\timport java.util.LinkedHashSet;\n    11\timport java.util.Set;\n    12\t\n    13\t/**\n    14\t * Startup check for the one class of deployment mistake that turns a correct component into remote\n    15\t * code execution: loading classes from somewhere another local account can write.\n    16\t *\n    17\t * <p>The component reads no files itself, so file permissions would be moot if it stayed alone. But\n    18\t * it does one thing that pulls the environment in: it discovers the application's session verifier\n    19\t * through a service loader, and a loader follows the classpath. If any location on that path is\n    20\t * writable by another account, that account can supply the code that answers \"is this session\n    21\t * valid\", and every guard in this package becomes decoration. Rule 8 asks for exactly this check, so\n    22\t * it is performed and enforced rather than assumed.\n    23\t *\n    24\t * <p>What is inspected: every JVM classpath entry, plus the code-source location (and its parent\n    25\t * directory, because a writable directory lets a file be replaced even when the file is not) of this\n    26\t * class and of the resolved verifier -- the two code bases that actually decide the outcome.\n    27\t *\n    28\t * <p>Descriptions name a path and nothing else. Paths are configuration, not secrets, and the text\n    29\t * stays terse because it reaches a log line.\n    30\t */\n    31\tfinal class DeploymentHygiene {\n    32\t\n    33\t    private static final int MAX_ENTRIES = 512;\n    34\t    private static final int MAX_PATH_LENGTH = 4096;\n    35\t\n    36\t    private DeploymentHygiene() {\n    37\t    }\n    38\t\n    39\t    /**\n    40\t     * @param codeOwners classes whose own code location must be trustworthy; typically this class and\n    41\t     *                   the resolved session verifier\n    42\t     * @return {@code null} when the deployment looks safe, otherwise a one-line reason to refuse\n    43\t     *         service. An unreadable location is reported as a problem: \"unknown\" is not \"safe\".\n    44\t     */\n    45\t    static String describeProblem(Class<?>... codeOwners) {\n    46\t        Set<Path> locations = new LinkedHashSet<Path>();\n    47\t        String classpath = System.getProperty(\"java.class.path\", \"\");\n    48\t        if (!classpath.isEmpty()) {\n    49\t            String[] entries = classpath.split(File.pathSeparator);\n    50\t            if (entries.length > MAX_ENTRIES) {\n    51\t                return \"the JVM classpath has more than \" + MAX_ENTRIES + \" entries\";\n    52\t            }\n    53\t            for (String entry : entries) {\n    54\t                Path path = toPath(entry);\n    55\t                if (path != null) {\n    56\t                    locations.add(path);\n    57\t                }\n    58\t            }\n    59\t        }\n    60\t        for (Class<?> codeOwner : codeOwners) {\n    61\t            if (codeOwner == null) {\n    62\t                return \"a required code owner was not supplied\";\n    63\t            }\n    64\t            Path fromCodeSource = codeSourceLocation(codeOwner);\n    65\t            if (fromCodeSource == null) {\n    66\t                // Cannot establish where this class was loaded from, so cannot prove it is not\n    67\t                // somebody else's to write. \"(unnamed location)\" : safe.toString();\n   111\t    }\n   112\t\n   113\t    private static Path toPath(String entry) {\n   114\t        if (entry == null || entry.isEmpty() || entry.length() > MAX_PATH_LENGTH) {\n   115\t            return null;\n   116\t        }\n   117\t        try {\n   118\t            return Paths.get(entry);\n   119\t        } catch (RuntimeException | Error unparseable) {\n   120\t            return null;\n   121\t        }\n   122\t    }\n   123\t\n   124\t    private static Path codeSourceLocation(Class<?> owner) {\n   125\t        try {\n   126\t            ProtectionDomain domain = owner.getProtectionDomain();\n   127\t            CodeSource source = domain == null ? null : domain.getCodeSource();\n   128\t            if (source == null || source.getLocation() == null) {\n   129\t                return null;\n   130\t            }\n   131\t            java.net.URL location = source.getLocation();\n   132\t            if (!\"file\".equalsIgnoreCase(location.getProtocol())) {\n   133\t                // A jar over http or a nested module location cannot be judged by POSIX bits, and\n   134\t                // neither is a directory an attacker could have written in place.\n   135\t                return null;\n   136\t            }\n   137\t            return toPath(java.net.URLDecoder.decode(location.getPath(),\n   138\t                    java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n   139\t        } catch (RuntimeException | Error unavailable) {\n   140\t            return null;\n   141\t        }\n   142\t    }\n   143\t\n   144\t    private static boolean worldWritable(Path path) {\n   145\t        try {\n   146\t            Set<java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission> permissions =\n   147\t                    Files.getPosixFilePermissions(path);\n   148\t            return permissions.contains(java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE);\n   149\t        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException notPosix) {\n   150\t            // A filesystem without POSIX bits cannot be judged here. Please start again.</p>\"\n    24\t            + \"</body></html>\";\n    25\t\n    26\t    /**\n    27\t     * Bound on the escaped <b>output</b>, so a long configured value cannot inflate a page.\n    28\t     *\n    29\t     * <p>The distinction matters, and this constant used to get it wrong by bounding the input\n    30\t     * instead: escaping only ever grows, so 256 characters of {@code \"} are 1 536 bytes of\n    31\t     * {@code &quot;} -- six times the limit, on a page whose guard had just reported itself satisfied.\n    32\t     * The loop therefore checks the room a character <i>needs</i> before emitting it, which keeps the\n    33\t     * result inside the bound and also guarantees it never cuts an entity in half: {@code &am} or\n    34\t     * {@code &quo} left in a document is broken markup, worse than a page that stops one entity early.\n    35\t     */\n    36\t    private static final int MAX_ESCAPED_LENGTH = 256;\n    37\t\n    38\t    /** Widest replacement this escaper can emit for a single input character ({@code &quot;}). */\n    39\t    private static final int WIDEST_ENTITY = 6;\n    40\t\n    41\t    /** Substitute for a control character; a space, never an NUL. */\n    42\t    private static final char SUBSTITUTE = (char) 0x20;\n    43\t\n    44\t    private ResponseBody() {\n    45\t    }\n    46\t\n    47\t    /**\n    48\t     * Success page. {@code null} or\n    60\t     *                         empty selects {@link #DEFAULT_SAME_SITE}\n    61\t     * @throws IllegalArgumentException if a value is malformed, or a {@code __Host-} name is paired\n    62\t     *         with a path the prefix contract forbids\n    63\t     */\n    64\t    public static SessionCookiePolicy of(String suppliedName, String suppliedPath, String suppliedSameSite) {\n    65\t        String name = orDefault(suppliedName, DEFAULT_NAME);\n    66\t        String path = orDefault(suppliedPath, DEFAULT_PATH);\n    67\t        String requestedSameSite = orDefault(suppliedSameSite, DEFAULT_SAME_SITE);\n    68\t\n    69\t        validateName(name);\n    70\t        validatePath(path);\n    71\t        String canonicalSameSite = canonicalSameSite(requestedSameSite);\n    72\t\n    73\t        // A __Host- name is a promise the browser enforces by dropping the cookie. A whitespace-only setting is a typo and is\n    86\t     * passed through for validation, where it is refused; values are never trimmed into validity.\n    87\t     */\n    88\t    private static String orDefault(String value, String fallback) {\n    89\t        if (value == null || value.isEmpty()) {\n    90\t            return fallback;\n    91\t        }\n    92\t        return value;\n    93\t    }\n    94\t\n    95\t    private static void validateName(String name) {\n    96\t        if (name.length() > MAX_NAME_LENGTH) {\n    97\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie name is longer than \" + MAX_NAME_LENGTH + \" characters\");\n    98\t        }\n    99\t        if (NAME_FIRST.indexOf(name.charAt(0)) < 0) {\n   100\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie name has an illegal first character\");\n   101\t        }\n   102\t        for (int i = 1; i < name.length(); i++) {\n   103\t            if (NAME_REST.indexOf(name.charAt(i)) < 0) {\n   104\t                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie name has an illegal character\");\n   105\t            }\n   106\t        }\n   107\t        if (RESERVED_NAMES.contains(name.toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT))) {\n   108\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie name collides with a protocol keyword\");\n   109\t        }\n   110\t    }\n   111\t\n   112\t    private static void validatePath(String path) {\n   113\t        if (path.length() > MAX_PATH_LENGTH) {\n   114\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie path is longer than \" + MAX_PATH_LENGTH + \" characters\");\n   115\t        }\n   116\t        if (path.charAt(0) != '/') {\n   117\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie path must start with '/'\");\n   118\t        }\n   119\t        for (int i = 0; i < path.length(); i++) {\n   120\t            char c = path.charAt(i);\n   121\t            boolean allowed = (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9')\n   122\t                    || c == '/' || c == '_' || c == '-' || c == '.';\n   123\t            if (!allowed) {\n   124\t                // Rejects ';', '\"', ',', ':', CR, LF, NUL, space and every non-ASCII byte, which is\n   125\t                // what stops a configured path injecting a cookie attribute or a header line.\n   126\t                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie path has an illegal character\");\n   127\t            }\n   128\t        }\n   129\t        if (path.indexOf(\"//\") >= 0 || path.indexOf(\"/./\") >= 0 || path.indexOf(\"/../\") >= 0) {\n   130\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\n   131\t                    \"cookie path must be normalized and must not contain '//' or dot segments\");\n   132\t        }\n   133\t    }\n   134\t\n   135\t    private static String canonicalSameSite(String value) {\n   136\t        if (\"strict\".equalsIgnoreCase(value)) {\n   137\t            return \"Strict\";\n   138\t        }\n   139\t        if (\"lax\".equalsIgnoreCase(value)) {\n   140\t            return \"Lax\";\n   141\t        }\n   142\t        if (\"none\".equalsIgnoreCase(value)) {\n   143\t            // SameSite=None is what a cross-site embed needs. It turns a first-party session cookie\n   144\t            // into a third-party cookie whose only remaining guard is Secure, and modern browsers\n   145\t            // increasingly drop it outright. Refused: an operator who really needs cross-site\n   146\t            // posting should make that call in code review, not in a properties file.\n   147\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"SameSite=None is refused; use Strict or Lax\");\n   148\t        }\n   149\t        throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"SameSite must be Strict or Lax\");\n   150\t    }\n   151\t\n   152\t    private SessionCookiePolicy(String name, String path, String sameSite) {\n   153\t        this.name = name;\n   154\t        this.path = path;\n   155\t        this.sameSite = sameSite;\n   156\t    }\n   157\t\n   158\t    public String name() {\n   159\t        return name;\n   160\t    }\n   161\t\n   162\t    public String path() {\n   163\t        return path;\n   164\t    }\n   165\t\n   166\t    public String sameSite() {\n   167\t        return sameSite;\n   168\t    }\n   169\t\n   170\t    /** Always true; see the class note on why this is not configuration. */\n   171\t    public boolean secure() {\n   172\t        return true;\n   173\t    }\n   174\t\n   175\t    /** Always true; see the class note on why this is not configuration. */\n   176\t    public boolean httpOnly() {\n   177\t        return true;\n   178\t    }\n   179\t\n   180\t    /** Never set: the cookie is host-only, so no other host can read it or shadow it. */\n   181\t    public String domain() {\n   182\t        return null;\n   183\t    }\n   184\t\n   185\t    /** {@code 0} means \"browser session only\": no Max-Age/Expires attribute is emitted. */\n   186\t    public int maxAge() {\n   187\t        return 0;\n   188\t    }\n   189\t\n   190\t    /** Attribute summary only; this type never holds session material. */\n   191\t    @Override\n   192\t    public String toString() {\n   193\t        return \"SessionCookiePolicy[name=\" + name + \", path=\" + path + \", sameSite=\" + sameSite\n   194\t                + \", secure=true, httpOnly=true, hostOnly=true, sessionCookie=true]\";\n   195\t    }\n   196\t}\nFILE src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionCookieWriter.java\n     1\tpackage com.acme.portal.session;\n     2\t\n     3\timport jakarta.servlet.http.Cookie;\n     4\timport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;\n     5\t\n     6\t/**\n     7\t * Emits the session cookie on a servlet response. This is the only class in the component that\n     8\t * touches a Servlet API type, so the security decision stays testable without a container.\n     9\t *\n    10\t * <p>The identifier is re-validated immediately before emission. {@link SessionId} already validated\n    11\t * it, but the value crosses a trust boundary at this line: a later maintainer could introduce a path\n    12\t * that hands this writer an identifier from somewhere else. The response is therefore only mutated\n    13\t * after the second check passes, and the check is reject-only (no normalisation), so a rejected value\n    14\t * cannot reach the response at all.\n    15\t */\n    16\tpublic final class SessionCookieWriter {\n    17\t\n    18\t    private static final String SAME_SITE_ATTRIBUTE = \"SameSite\";\n    19\t\n    20\t    /** Any response that can carry session material is uncacheable, without exception. */\n    21\t    private static final String NO_STORE = \"no-store, max-age=0\";\n    22\t\n    23\t    private final SessionCookiePolicy policy;\n    24\t\n    25\t    public SessionCookieWriter(SessionCookiePolicy policy) {\n    26\t        if (policy == null) {\n    27\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie policy is required\");\n    28\t        }\n    29\t        this.policy = policy;\n    30\t    }\n    31\t\n    32\t    /**\n    33\t     * Writes the anti-caching headers and then the {@code Set-Cookie} header. Nothing else.\n    34\t     *\n    35\t     * <p>{@code no-store} is set first so that if the cookie write below somehow fails, the partial\n    36\t     * response that reaches the network is already uncacheable. Both must precede the first body byte:\n    37\t     * a servlet container silently discards headers set afterwards.\n    38\t     *\n    39\t     * @param sessionIdentifier identifier already accepted by the trusted session owner\n    40\t     * @param response          response to mutate\n    41\t     * @throws InvalidSessionIdentifierException if the identifier is not a well-formed token; the\n    42\t     *         response then carries the cache headers and no cookie\n    43\t     */\n    44\t    public void writeSessionCookie(SessionId sessionIdentifier, HttpServletResponse response)\n    45\t            throws InvalidSessionIdentifierException {\n    46\t        if (response == null) {\n    47\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"response is required\");\n    48\t        }\n    49\t        // Unconditional and first: a response that carried, failed to carry, or partially carried\n    50\t        // session material must never be storable by a browser or an intermediary.\n    51\t        response.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", NO_STORE);\n    52\t        response.setHeader(\"Pragma\", \"no-cache\");\n    53\t        if (sessionIdentifier == null) {\n    54\t            throw new InvalidSessionIdentifierException(SessionId.Rejection.ABSENT);\n    55\t        }\n    56\t        String value = sessionIdentifier.value();\n    57\t        SessionId.Rejection rejection = SessionId.inspect(value);\n    58\t        if (rejection != SessionId.Rejection.NONE) {\n    59\t            // The reason class is safe to log. The value is interpolated nowhere below.\n    60\t            throw new InvalidSessionIdentifierException(rejection);\n    61\t        }\n    62\t\n    63\t        Cookie cookie = new Cookie(policy.name(), value);\n    64\t        cookie.setPath(policy.path());\n    65\t        cookie.setHttpOnly(policy.httpOnly());\n    66\t        cookie.setSecure(policy.secure());\n    67\t        // setMaxAge is deliberately never called. Tomcat renders maxAge == 0 as\n    68\t        // \"Expires=Tue Nov 17 10:15:05 GMT 1995\", an already-expired cookie the browser discards, so\n    69\t        // a session cookie must keep the API default of emitting no expiry attribute at all.\n    70\t        // Servlet 6.0 has no SameSite setter; the attribute map is the supported extension point and\n    71\t        // Tomcat 10.1 renders it into Set-Cookie. Host-only by deliberately never calling setDomain.\n    72\t        cookie.setAttribute(SAME_SITE_ATTRIBUTE, policy.sameSite());\n    73\t        // addCookie, not setHeader: the container owns header syntax and quoting. One call emits one\n    74\t        // Set-Cookie, so there is no header-injection surface even if a name were ever wrong.\n    75\t        response.addCookie(cookie);\n    76\t    }\n    77\t\n    78\t    /** The policy in force; for startup logging and for the caller's success page. */\n    79\t    public SessionCookiePolicy policy() {\n    80\t        return policy;\n    81\t    }\n    82\t}\nFILE src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionHandoffService.java\n     1\tpackage com.acme.portal.session;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * The security decision of this component, with no servlet types in it: given what the trusted\n     5\t * session owner asserted and what the client sent, may a session cookie be issued?\n     6\t *\n     7\t * <p>Deliberately free of container and I/O dependencies, so the decision can be exercised directly\n     8\t * in tests and the HTTP layer stays a thin adapter that is safe to replace.\n     9\t */\n    10\tpublic final class SessionHandoffService {\n    11\t\n    12\t    /** Result of one handoff attempt. */\n    13\t    public enum Outcome {\n    14\t        /** A session cookie may be issued; see {@link Decision#sessionIdentifier()}. */\n    15\t        ISSUED,\n    16\t        /** The assertion was absent or malformed; nothing was looked up and no allowance consumed. */\n    17\t        REFUSED_INVALID_ASSERTION,\n    18\t        /** The identifier is well-formed but not confirmed by the authoritative session store. */\n    19\t        REFUSED_UNAUTHENTICATED,\n    20\t        /** This source used up its window allowance. */\n    21\t        LIMITED_SOURCE,\n    22\t        /** The shared lane is saturated; this source's own allowance is untouched. */\n    23\t        LIMITED_GLOBAL,\n    24\t        /** Too many distinct sources to track; shedding load. */\n    25\t        LIMITED_TRACKING,\n    26\t        /** The component is not serving. */\n    27\t        REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE\n    28\t    }\n    29\t\n    30\t    /** The answer, with no session material in its {@code toString()}. */\n    31\t    public static final class Decision {\n    32\t        private final Outcome outcome;\n    33\t        private final SessionId sessionId;\n    34\t        private final int retryAfterSeconds;\n    35\t        private final SessionId.Rejection rejection;\n    36\t\n    37\t        private Decision(Outcome outcome, SessionId sessionId, int retryAfterSeconds) {\n    38\t            this(outcome, sessionId, retryAfterSeconds, SessionId.Rejection.NONE);\n    39\t        }\n    40\t\n    41\t        private Decision(Outcome outcome, SessionId sessionId, int retryAfterSeconds,\n    42\t                         SessionId.Rejection rejection) {\n    43\t            this.outcome = outcome;\n    44\t            this.sessionId = sessionId;\n    45\t            this.retryAfterSeconds = retryAfterSeconds;\n    46\t            // A name from a fixed enum, never the value: this is the one detail the caller may log\n    47\t            // about a rejected assertion, and it is why the reason is carried at all.\n    48\t            this.rejection = rejection == null ? SessionId.Rejection.NONE : rejection;\n    49\t        }\n    50\t\n    51\t        public Outcome outcome() {\n    52\t            return outcome;\n    53\t        }\n    54\t\n    55\t        /**\n    56\t         * Which validation rule rejected the asserted identifier. Package private on purpose: it\n    57\t         * exists so the HTTP adapter can write a diagnosable log line, not so an outsider can\n    58\t         * enumerate why assertions fail. Everything the client can observe stays one generic 403\n    59\t         * either way (rule 6), so this trades nothing for the operator's ability to tell \"the\n    60\t         * authenticator asserted nothing\" apart from \"the assertion was 31 characters\".\n    61\t         */\n    62\t        SessionId.Rejection rejectionReason() {\n    63\t            return rejection;\n    64\t        }\n    65\t\n    66\t        /**\n    67\t         * The validated identifier to place in the cookie.
