Implementations should return {@code false} rather than throw for \"no\"; a throw\n    93\t     * is still handled as \"no\" below.\n    94\t     */\n    95\t    public interface SessionStateVerifier {\n    96\t        boolean isSessionValid(String sessionId);\n    97\t    }\n    98\t\n    99\t    /** A verifier that confirms nothing, so a missing integration fails closed. */\n   100\t    public static final SessionStateVerifier DENY_ALL = new SessionStateVerifier() {\n   101\t        @Override\n   102\t        public boolean isSessionValid(String sessionId) {\n   103\t            return false;\n   104\t        }\n   105\t    };\n   106\t\n   107\t    /** Where the assertion comes from. Everything here is attacker-influenced until validated. */\n   108\t    public static final class Request {\n   109\t        private final String assertedSessionId;\n   110\t        private final ClientAddress source;\n   111\t\n   112\t        public Request(String assertedSessionId, ClientAddress source) {\n   113\t            this.assertedSessionId = assertedSessionId;\n   114\t            this.source = source;\n   115\t        }\n   116\t    }\n   117\t\n   118\t    private final SessionCookiePolicy policy;\n   119\t    private final SessionStateVerifier verifier;\n   120\t    private final AttemptLimiter limiter;\n   121\t\n   122\t    public SessionHandoffService(SessionCookiePolicy policy, SessionStateVerifier verifier,\n   123\t                                 AttemptLimiter limiter) {\n   124\t        if (policy == null || limiter == null) {\n   125\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie policy and limiter are required\");\n   126\t        }\n   127\t        // A null verifier is not \"no verification needed\", it is \"nothing can be verified\", so the\n   128\t        // only safe behaviour is to refuse every handoff. Failing open here would mean issuing a\n   129\t        // session cookie for any string a client could invent.\n   130\t        this.policy = policy;\n   131\t        this.verifier = verifier == null ? DENY_ALL : verifier;\n   132\t        this.limiter = limiter;\n   133\t    }\n   134\t\n   135\t    /**\n   136\t     * Ordered so that no cheap unauthenticated check can be used to probe, and no failure path can\n   137\t     * reach a cookie write.\n   138\t     *\n   139\t     * <ol>\n   140\t     *   <li><b>Validate the asserted identifier.</b> Cheap, no I/O, and it guarantees a malformed\n   141\t     *       string is never handed to another subsystem as though it were a lookup key.</li>\n   142\t     *   <li><b>Consume rate-limit allowance.</b> Only well-formed requests pay for it, so an attacker\n   143\t     *       cannot exhaust a victim's allowance by sending garbage; and the session-store lookup below\n   144\t     *       sits behind this gate, which is what stops the verifier being used as an amplifier. Two\n   145\t     *       limits on that claim, stated rather than glossed: an attempt the store then refuses has\n   146\t     *       still consumed its allowance, because it was a real authentication attempt and making\n   147\t     *       those free would hand an attacker free probing; and the fixed timing padding charged by\n   148\t     *       the HTTP adapter runs <i>before</i> this gate, so what this gate sheds is the store round\n   149\t     *       trip and the cookie write, not every byte of work a request costs.</li>\n   150\t     *   <li><b>Verify against server-side state.</b> The only step that can authorise a cookie.</li>\n   151\t     * </ol>\n   152\t     */\n   153\t    public Decision handle(Request request) {\n   154\t        if (request == null) {\n   155\t            return new Decision(Outcome.REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE, null, 0);\n   156\t        }\n   157\t        ClientAddress source = request.source == null ? ClientAddress.UNKNOWN : request.source;\n   158\t\n   159\t        SessionId sessionId;\n   160\t        try {\n   161\t            sessionId = SessionId.fromTrustedComponent(request.assertedSessionId);\n   162\t        } catch (InvalidSessionIdentifierException rejected) {\n   163\t            // The reason class is loggable; the value is not. Logging is the caller's job and is\n   164\t            // deliberately not done here, so a log level cannot become a disclosure path. The reason\n   165\t            // travels with the decision because \"the authenticator asserted nothing\" and \"the\n   166\t            // authenticator asserted a 31-character string\" are different bugs to fix, and the\n   167\t            // response the client sees is identical for both either way.\n   168\t            return new Decision(Outcome.REFUSED_INVALID_ASSERTION, null, 0, rejected.rejection());\n   169\t        }\n   170\t\n   171\t        AttemptLimiter.Decision budget = limiter.tryAcquire(source);\n   172\t        if (budget == AttemptLimiter.Decision.LIMITED_PER_SOURCE) {\n   173\t            return new Decision(Outcome.LIMITED_SOURCE, null, limiter.retryAfterSeconds(source));\n   174\t        }\n   175\t        if (budget == AttemptLimiter.Decision.LIMITED_GLOBALLY) {\n   176\t            return new Decision(Outcome.LIMITED_GLOBAL, null, limiter.retryAfterSeconds(source));\n   177\t        }\n   178\t        if (budget == AttemptLimiter.Decision.LIMITED_TRACKING_SATURATED) {\n   179\t            return new Decision(Outcome.LIMITED_TRACKING, null, limiter.retryAfterSeconds(source));\n   180\t        }\n   181\t\n   182\t        boolean valid;\n   183\t        try {\n   184\t            valid = verifier.isSessionValid(sessionId.value());\n   185\t        } catch (RuntimeException verifierFailed) {\n   186\t            // A verifier that cannot answer did not confirm anything. Treat it as \"no\", and let the\n   187\t            // caller log only the exception type: a cause message from a datastore can carry a\n   188\t            // connection string, and this component must not put one on the console.\n   189\t            valid = false;\n   190\t        }\n   191\t        if (!valid) {\n   192\t            return new Decision(Outcome.REFUSED_UNAUTHENTICATED, null, 0);\n   193\t        }\n   194\t        return new Decision(Outcome.ISSUED, sessionId, 0);\n   195\t    }\n   196\t\n   197\t    /** The policy in force, for startup logging. */\n   198\t    public SessionCookiePolicy policy() {\n   199\t        return policy;\n   200\t    }\n   201\t\n   202\t    /** Configuration summary; contains no secret and no client address. */\n   203\t    @Override\n   204\t    public String toString() {\n   205\t        return \"SessionHandoffService[\" + policy + \", \" + limiter + \"]\";\n   206\t    }\n   207\t}\nFILE src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionHandoffServlet.java\n     1\tpackage com.acme.portal.session;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.io.IOException;\n     4\timport java.io.PrintWriter;\n     5\timport java.util.logging.Level;\n     6\timport java.util.logging.Logger;\n     7\t\n     8\timport jakarta.servlet.ServletException;\n     9\timport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;\n    10\timport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;\n    11\t\n    12\t/**\n    13\t * HTTP adapter for the session-handoff decision.\n    14\t *\n    15\t * <p><b>It never creates, reads or invalidates a servlet session.</b> No call to\n    16\t * {@code getRequestSession} appears in this class, deliberately: a container session would be a\n    17\t * second, parallel session mechanism with its own {@code Set-Cookie} and its own cookie name, and the\n    18\t * whole point of this component is to emit exactly one session cookie whose attributes it controls.\n    19\t * The identifier it issues comes from the trusted component's own store.\n    20\t *\n    21\t * <p><b>Inputs, all treated as attacker-controlled.</b> The method, the request URI, the source\n    22\t * address and {@code If-None-Match} come from the client. The asserted session identifier comes from\n    23\t * server-side code upstream in this JVM, and is <i>still</i> validated and re-checked against the\n    24\t * authoritative store, because a filter ordering mistake or a compromised upstream must not be able\n    25\t * to mint a cookie from a client-chosen string. Cookies, query parameters, body fields and every\n    26\t * {@code *-For} header are never read.\n    27\t *\n    28\t * <p><b>Response shape.</b> Exactly two bodies, both constant. Two status families: a refusal that\n    29\t * is an authentication outcome (403) and a refusal that is availability (429/503), which is the\n    30\t * smallest distinction that stays honest -- a 403 says \"no session was issued to you\", which is true\n    31\t * and observable from the missing cookie anyway, and never says why.\n    32\t *\n    33\t * <p>Servlet 6.0 has no {@code SC_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS} constant (it arrived in 6.1), hence the local\n    34\t * one.\n    35\t */\n    36\tpublic final class SessionHandoffServlet extends jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServlet {\n    37\t\n    38\t    private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n    39\t\n    40\t    /** Added in Servlet 6.1; declared here so this compiles and runs on 6.0. */\n    41\t    static final int SC_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS = 429;\n    42\t    /** Not in the Servlet API constants either. */\n    43\t    static final int SC_NOT_IMPLEMENTED = 501;\n    44\t\n    45\t    private static final Logger LOG = Logger.getLogger(SessionHandoffServlet.class.getName());\n    46\t\n    47\t    /**\n    48\t     * Collaborators, written by the container's {@code init()} thread and read by every request\n    49\t     * thread afterwards.\n    50\t     *\n    51\t     * <p>All four are {@code volatile}, and {@link #serving} is written <i>last</i> in\n    52\t     * {@link #configure} and {@code serveNothing()}. That pairing is the point: a request thread that\n    53\t     * observes {@code serving == true} performs an acquire read of the same field the wiring thread\n    54\t     * released, so it must also see the three references written before it, and one that observes\n    55\t     * {@code false} never reads them at all. Without it the container's \"init finished before service\n    56\t     * ran\" ordering is a spec promise rather than a happens-before this class can rely on: a worker\n    57\t     * could see {@code serving == true} next to a {@code null} collaborator, and the NPE that follows\n    58\t     * is caught and answered as a 503 -- a refusal, so nothing unsafe escapes, but a self-inflicted\n    59\t     * outage during the first requests after a deploy, which is exactly when an operator is watching.\n    60\t     * Rule 7 asks for check-and-act atomicity; for a wire-once/read-many object that is visibility,\n    61\t     * not locking, and volatile gets it without a monitor on the hot path.\n    62\t     */\n    63\t    private transient volatile SessionHandoffService service;\n    64\t    private transient volatile SessionCookieWriter cookieWriter;\n    65\t    private transient volatile TrustedSessionAssertionSource assertionSource;\n    66\t    private transient volatile boolean serving;\n    67\t\n    68\t    /**\n    69\t     * Wires the component from validated environment settings and the session verifier the\n    70\t     * application contributes through {@link SessionStateVerifierProvider}.\n    71\t     *\n    72\t     * <p>Fail-closed by construction: invalid settings, an absent verifier or unsafe deployment\n    73\t     * hygiene leave the servlet refusing every request with 503. A servlet that cannot verify a\n    74\t     * session must never fall back to \"assume the authenticator was right\".\n    75\t     */\n    76\t    @Override\n    77\t    public void init() {\n    78\t        SessionHandoffSettings settings;\n    79\t        try {\n    80\t            settings = SessionHandoffSettings.fromEnvironment();\n    81\t        } catch (IllegalArgumentException badSettings) {\n    82\t            // The message names a setting and a violated bound, never a value read from the\n    83\t            // environment, so it is safe to log.\n    84\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: {0}\", badSettings.getMessage());\n    85\t            serveNothing();\n    86\t            return;\n    87\t        }\n    88\t        String hygieneProblem;\n    89\t        try {\n    90\t            // Checked before anything is discovered: this component is about to load a class it does\n    91\t            // not own, so its own code base and the classpath must already be proven read-only.\n    92\t            hygieneProblem = DeploymentHygiene.describeProblem(SessionHandoffServlet.class);\n    93\t        } catch (RuntimeException hygieneCheckFailed) {\n    94\t            hygieneProblem = \"the deployment hygiene check failed: \"\n    95\t                    + hygieneCheckFailed.getClass().getName();\n    96\t        }\n    97\t        if (hygieneProblem != null) {\n    98\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: {0}\", hygieneProblem);\n    99\t            serveNothing();\n   100\t            return;\n   101\t        }\n   102\t        SessionHandoffService.SessionStateVerifier verifier;\n   103\t        try {\n   104\t            verifier = SessionStateVerifierProvider.load();\n   105\t        } catch (RuntimeException providerFailed) {\n   106\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: the session verifier could not be\"\n   107\t                    + \" loaded, exceptionType={0}\", providerFailed.getClass().getName());\n   108\t            serveNothing();\n   109\t            return;\n   110\t        }\n   111\t        if (verifier == null) {\n   112\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: no session verifier is deployed\");\n   113\t            serveNothing();\n   114\t            return;\n   115\t        }\n   116\t        try {\n   117\t            // Re-checked with the verifier's own code base in scope, because that class is the\n   118\t            // authority on whether a session exists.\n   119\t            hygieneProblem = DeploymentHygiene.describeProblem(SessionHandoffServlet.class, verifier.getClass());\n   120\t        } catch (RuntimeException hygieneCheckFailed) {\n   121\t            hygieneProblem = \"the deployment hygiene check failed: \"\n   122\t                    + hygieneCheckFailed.getClass().getName();\n   123\t        }\n   124\t        if (hygieneProblem != null) {\n   125\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: {0}\", hygieneProblem);\n   126\t            serveNothing();\n   127\t            return;\n   128\t        }\n   129\t        SessionCookieWriter writer = new SessionCookieWriter(settings.cookiePolicy());\n   130\t        configure(new SessionHandoffService(settings.cookiePolicy(), verifier, settings.limiter()),\n   131\t                writer, new RequestAttributeAssertionSource(settings.assertionAttribute()));\n   132\t    }\n   133\t\n   134\t    private void serveNothing() {\n   135\t        this.service = null;\n   136\t        this.cookieWriter = null;\n   137\t        this.assertionSource = null;\n   138\t        this.serving = false;\n   139\t    }\n   140\t\n   141\t    /**\n   142\t     * Wires the component explicitly. Every argument is required: a partially wired servlet would be\n   143\t     * an unauthenticated cookie issuer, so a null here means \"do not serve\" rather than \"do less\n   144\t     * checking\".\n   145\t     */\n   146\t    public void configure(SessionHandoffService service, SessionCookieWriter cookieWriter,\n   147\t                          TrustedSessionAssertionSource assertionSource) {\n   148\t        boolean complete = service != null && cookieWriter != null && assertionSource != null;\n   149\t        if (!complete) {\n   150\t            serveNothing();\n   151\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: required collaborators are missing\");\n   152\t            return;\n   153\t        }\n   154\t        this.service = service;\n   155\t        this.cookieWriter = cookieWriter;\n   156\t        this.assertionSource = assertionSource;\n   157\t        this.serving = true;\n   158\t        // Policy and limits only. No identifier, no address, no secret.\n   159\t        LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff ready: {0} wired with {1}\",\n   160\t                new Object[]{service, cookieWriter.policy()});\n   161\t    }\n   162\t\n   163\t    @Override\n   164\t    protected void doGet(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response)\n   165\t            throws ServletException, IOException {\n   166\t        handle(request, response, true);\n   167\t    }\n   168\t\n   169\t    @Override\n   170\t    protected void doHead(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response)\n   171\t            throws ServletException, IOException {\n   172\t        // HEAD takes the same decision path, the same status codes, the same headers and the same\n   173\t        // fixed work as GET, and writes no body. It is deliberately not a cheaper or freer request: it\n   174\t        // consumes rate-limit allowance and asks the session store its question, because answering it\n   175\t        // anywhere earlier would create exactly the cheap probe of session existence that GET is not\n   176\t        // allowed to be.\n   177\t        //\n   178\t        // What it does not do is issue a cookie. A HEAD response body is discarded by definition, so a\n   179\t        // credential handed to one cannot be used by the client that asked for it, while remaining\n   180\t        // perfectly visible to the prefetchers, link scanners and proxies that send HEADs and log\n   181\t        // them. A scanner would also consume a real user's allowance on requests that can never carry\n   182\t        // the credential away. The 200 that answers a successful HEAD is not a credential and not a\n   183\t        // quiet channel either: it can only be elicited by a request the authenticator has already\n   184\t        // asserted a session for, it travels only to the client that made it, and it is logged with\n   185\t        // the session fingerprint like any other grant.\n   186\t        handle(request, response, false);\n   187\t    }\n   188\t\n   189\t    @Override\n   190\t    protected void doPost(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   191\t        refuseMethod(response);\n   192\t    }\n   193\t\n   194\t    @Override\n   195\t    protected void doPut(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   196\t        refuseMethod(response);\n   197\t    }\n   198\t\n   199\t    @Override\n   200\t    protected void doDelete(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   201\t        refuseMethod(response);\n   202\t    }\n   203\t\n   204\t    @Override\n   205\t    protected void doOptions(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   206\t        refuseMethod(response);\n   207\t    }\n   208\t\n   209\t    @Override\n   210\t    protected void doTrace(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   211\t        // TRACE is disabled outright: it echoes request headers back to the caller, which is how\n   212\t        // cross-site tracing reads a session cookie.\n   213\t        response.setStatus(SC_NOT_IMPLEMENTED);\n   214\t        response.setHeader(\"Allow\", \"GET, HEAD\");\n   215\t    }\n   216\t\n   217\t    private void refuseMethod(HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   218\t        response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED);\n   219\t        response.setHeader(\"Allow\", \"GET, HEAD\");\n   220\t        response.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-store\");\n   221\t        // No body: the status is the whole answer, and a body would need an escaping decision.\n   222\t        response.flushBuffer();\n   223\t    }\n   224\t\n   225\t    /**\n   226\t     * The single decision path. {@code writeBody} is false for HEAD.\n   227\t     *\n   228\t     * <p>Every header is set before the first body byte, and the body is written last, because a\n   229\t     * servlet container silently discards headers set afterwards -- a dropped {@code Set-Cookie} or\n   230\t     * {@code Retry-After} would be invisible.\n   231\t     */\n   232\t    private void handle(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response, boolean writeBody)\n   233\t            throws IOException {\n   234\t        if (response == null) {\n   235\t            return;\n   236\t        }\n   237\t        // Applied once, on every request, before the decision is taken -- including the requests the\n   238\t        // throttle and the availability check turn away. Padding only the refusal paths is not enough:\n   239\t        // a path that costs nothing next to a path that costs tens of microseconds is itself the\n   240\t        // oracle an attacker reads. Equal cost for grant and every refusal alike is what removes the\n   241\t        // \"does this session exist\" signal. The cost of that, and why the throttle therefore sheds\n   242\t        // the session-store round trip rather than this, is written out in WorkEqualizer.\n   243\t        WorkEqualizer.apply();\n   244\t        try {\n   245\t            if (request == null) {\n   246\t                // Cannot happen through the container; guarded so a direct call cannot NPE into a\n   247\t                // 200-with-nothing.\n   248\t                respondUnavailable(response, writeBody);\n   249\t                return;\n   250\t            }\n   251\t            if (!serving) {\n   252\t                respondUnavailable(response, writeBody);\n   253\t                return;\n   254\t            }\n   255\t\n   256\t            ClientAddress source = ClientAddress.parse(request.getRemoteAddr());\n   257\t            String assertedId = readAssertion(request);\n   258\t            SessionHandoffService.Decision decision =\n   259\t                    service.handle(new SessionHandoffService.Request(assertedId, source));\n   260\t\n   261\t            switch (decision.outcome()) {\n   262\t                case ISSUED:\n   263\t                    respondGranted(response, decision, writeBody);\n   264\t                    return;\n   265\t                case LIMITED_SOURCE:\n   266\t                case LIMITED_GLOBAL:\n   267\t                case LIMITED_TRACKING:\n   268\t                    respondLimited(response, decision, writeBody);\n   269\t                    // Only the outcome and the bucket shape. Never an identifier, never the address.\n   270\t                    LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff rate limited, outcome={0}, unknownSource={1}\",\n   271\t                            new Object[]{decision.outcome(), Boolean.valueOf(source.isUnknown())});\n   272\t                    return;\n   273\t                case REFUSED_INVALID_ASSERTION:\n   274\t                    respondRefused(response, writeBody);\n   275\t                    // The reason enum and nothing else: it tells an operator whether the authenticator\n   276\t                    // asserted nothing at all or asserted something the validator refused, which are\n   277\t                    // different bugs to chase, while the value itself stays out of the log (rule 5).\n   278\t                    LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff refused: the authenticator asserted no usable\"\n   279\t                            + \" session identifier, reason={0}\", decision.rejectionReason().name());\n   280\t                    return;\n   281\t                case REFUSED_UNAUTHENTICATED:\n   282\t                case REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE:\n   283\t                default:\n   284\t                    respondRefused(response, writeBody);\n   285\t                    LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff refused: the session store did not confirm the\"\n   286\t                            + \" asserted identifier\");\n   287\t                    return;\n   288\t            }\n   289\t        } catch (RuntimeException | IOException unexpected) {\n   290\t            // Never propagate: the container's error page would disclose class names, paths and\n   291\t            // versions. Log the exception type only -- a datastore or JDBC message can carry a\n   292\t            // connection string, and this component must not put one on any log it does not own.\n   293\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff aborted, exceptionType={0}\",\n   294\t                    unexpected.getClass().getName());\n   295\t            respondUnavailable(response, writeBody);\n   296\t        }\n   297\t    }\n   298\t\n   299\t    /**\n   300\t     * Reads the assertion through the injected source, which is contractually forbidden from looking\n   301\t     * at client data. A throw is \"no assertion\", logged as a type only.\n   302\t     */\n   303\t    private String readAssertion(HttpServletRequest request) {\n   304\t        try {\n   305\t            return assertionSource.assertedSessionId(request);\n   306\t        } catch (RuntimeException sourceFailed) {\n   307\t            LOG.log(Level.WARNING, \"assertion source failed, exceptionType={0}\",\n   308\t                    sourceFailed.getClass().getName());\n   309\t            return null;\n   310\t        }\n   311\t    }\n   312\t\n   313\t    private void respondGranted(HttpServletResponse response, SessionHandoffService.Decision decision,\n   314\t                                boolean writeBody) throws IOException {\n   315\t        String body = ResponseBody.granted(cookieWriter.policy());\n   316\t        // Emitted before the cookie rather than after it: if the write below throws, the half response\n   317\t        // the client is left holding is already uncacheable.\n   318\t        applyStandardHeaders(response);\n   319\t        if (!writeBody) {\n   320\t            // HEAD is answered from the same decision, through the same header block and the same\n   321\t            // measure-the-body step as the GET that would answer it, minus the Set-Cookie it never\n   322\t            // issues. Content-Length reports the body the GET would have produced, which is what a HEAD\n   323\t            // is for. A method whose responses have a different header shape is a second, quieter way\n   324\t            // to tell two outcomes apart, so HEAD shares this code rather than imitating it in a second\n   325\t            // copy that could drift; the harness asserts the equality for the grant and every refusal.\n   326\t            response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_OK);\n   327\t            writeOrMeasure(response, body, false);\n   328\t            LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff availability check passed for session {0}\",\n   329\t                    decision.sessionIdentifier().fingerprint());\n   330\t            return;\n   331\t        }\n   332\t        try {\n   333\t            // The writer emits its own Cache-Control/Pragma (same values, set again is a no-op) and\n   334\t            // then the cookie, so a partial response is uncacheable before the value appears.\n   335\t            cookieWriter.writeSessionCookie(decision.sessionIdentifier(), response);\n   336\t        } catch (InvalidSessionIdentifierException rejectedAtWrite) {\n   337\t            // The writer's independent gate disagreed with the service. That should be impossible,\n   338\t            // and it is still a refusal rather than a judgement call: no cookie, generic body.\n   339\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff refused at the cookie gate, reason={0}\",\n   340\t                    rejectedAtWrite.rejection().name());\n   341\t            respondRefused(response, writeBody);\n   342\t            return;\n   343\t        }\n   344\t        response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_OK);\n   345\t        writeOrMeasure(response, body, true);\n   346\t        LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff granted for session {0}\",\n   347\t                decision.sessionIdentifier().fingerprint());\n   348\t    }\n   349\t\n   350\t    private void respondRefused(HttpServletResponse response, boolean writeBody) throws IOException {\n   351\t        response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_FORBIDDEN);\n   352\t        applyStandardHeaders(response);\n   353\t        writeOrMeasure(response, ResponseBody.REFUSAL, writeBody);\n   354\t    }\n   355\t\n   356\t    private void respondLimited(HttpServletResponse response, SessionHandoffService.Decision decision,\n   357\t                                boolean writeBody) throws IOException {\n   358\t        response.setStatus(SC_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS);\n   359\t        applyStandardHeaders(response);\n   360\t        int retryAfter = decision.retryAfterSeconds();\n   361\t        if (retryAfter > 0) {\n   362\t            response.setIntHeader(\"Retry-After\", retryAfter);\n   363\t        }\n   364\t        writeOrMeasure(response, ResponseBody.REFUSAL, writeBody);\n   365\t    }\n   366\t\n   367\t    private void respondUnavailable(HttpServletResponse response, boolean writeBody) throws IOException {\n   368\t        response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE);\n   369\t        applyStandardHeaders(response);\n   370\t        // Short, so a load balancer retries rather than marking the backend down for a long time.\n   371\t        response.setIntHeader(\"Retry-After\", 5);\n   372\t        writeOrMeasure(response, ResponseBody.REFUSAL, writeBody);\n   373\t    }\n   374\t\n   375\t    /**\n   376\t     * The header block every response carries, whatever the status and whatever the method.\n   377\t     *\n   378\t     * <p>One method rather than four copies: the cache directives, content type and {@code nosniff} are\n   379\t     * the response's <i>shape</i>, and the shape is what must not encode anything about the decision\n   380\t     * behind it. Every copy added is another place that can drift, and drift here is exactly the\n   381\t     * outcome-telling side channel the rest of this class works to remove -- a response that is\n   382\t     * cacheable, or plain-text, or of a different length class, says which gate answered. The harness\n   383\t     * asserts the equality across grant, refusal, limit and unavailable, and across GET and HEAD.\n   384\t     */\n   385\t    private void applyStandardHeaders(HttpServletResponse response) {\n   386\t        // Pragma for HTTP/1.0 proxies, max-age=0 for anything that honours Cache-Control strictly.\n   387\t        response.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-store, max-age=0\");\n   388\t        response.setHeader(\"Pragma\", \"no-cache\");\n   389\t        response.setContentType(\"text/html;charset=UTF-8\");\n   390\t        response.setHeader(\"X-Content-Type-Options\", \"nosniff\");\n   391\t    }\n   392\t\n   393\t    /**\n   394\t     * Writes {@code body} for a GET; for a HEAD reports the length that body would have had.\n   395\t     *\n   396\t     * <p>Not just RFC tidiness. If the refusals answered a HEAD with no {@code Content-Length} while\n   397\t     * the grant answered one with a length, the header shape alone would separate the outcomes for a\n   398\t     * client that sent HEAD instead of GET -- a second, quieter version of the body-tell this class\n   399\t     * goes out of its way not to have. Both methods now describe the same document.\n   400\t     */\n   401\t    private void writeOrMeasure(HttpServletResponse response, String body, boolean writeBody)\n   402\t            throws IOException {\n   403\t        if (writeBody) {\n   404\t            write(response, body);\n   405\t        } else {\n   406\t            response.setContentLength(body.getBytes(java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.UTF_8).length);\n   407\t            response.flushBuffer();\n   408\t        }\n   409\t    }\n   410\t\n   411\t    private void write(HttpServletResponse response, String body) throws IOException {\n   412\t        PrintWriter out = response.getWriter();\n   413\t        out.write(body);\n   414\t        out.flush();\n   415\t    }\n   416\t\n   417\t    /** Configuration summary for an operator endpoint; never contains a secret or an identifier. */\n   418\t    @Override\n   419\t    public String toString() {\n   420\t        return \"SessionHandoffServlet[serving=\" + serving\n   421\t                + (cookieWriter == null ? \"\" : \", \" + cookieWriter.policy()) + \"]\";\n   422\t    }\n   423\t\n   424\t    /** Used by the deployment descriptor check and the harness. */\n   425\t    boolean isServing() {\n   426\t        return serving;\n   427\t    }\n   428\t}\nFILE src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionHandoffSettings.java\n     1\tpackage com.acme.portal.session;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Collections;\n     4\timport java.util.LinkedHashMap;\n     5\timport java.util.Map;\n     6\t\n     7\t/**\n     8\t * Validated runtime settings for the handoff component.\n     9\t *\n    10\t * <p>Environment variables are configuration, and configuration is attacker-influenced in the\n    11\t * general case: a sidecar, a Helm value, a {@code .env} file or a CI variable can all change them\n    12\t * without anyone reviewing Java code. Every value read here is therefore validated for type, length\n    13\t * and range before use, and an invalid value aborts startup.\n    14\t *\n    15\t * <p>Defaults exist only for the availability knobs, and every default is the stricter choice\n    16\t * (short window, low ceiling) rather than a permissive one. Nothing security-relevant is defaulted:\n    17\t * {@code Secure}, {@code HttpOnly}, host-only scope and session-cookie lifetime are invariants of\n    18\t * {@link SessionCookiePolicy} and cannot be turned off from here.\n    19\t *\n    20\t * <p>No secret is read, held or derivable from this class. There is no signing key because a\n    21\t * session cookie carries an opaque identifier that the authoritative store resolves, so nothing needs\n    22\t * to be proven by a MAC; adding one would add a secret to protect for no security gain.\n    23\t */\n    24\tpublic final class SessionHandoffSettings {\n    25\t\n    26\t    public static final String COOKIE_NAME_ENV = \"PORTAL_COOKIE_NAME\";\n    27\t    public static final String COOKIE_PATH_ENV = \"PORTAL_COOKIE_PATH\";\n    28\t    public static final String SAME_SITE_ENV = \"PORTAL_COOKIE_SAME_SITE\";\n    29\t    public static final String WINDOW_SECONDS_ENV = \"PORTAL_HANDOFF_WINDOW_SECONDS\";\n    30\t    public static final String MAX_PER_WINDOW_ENV = \"PORTAL_HANDOFF_MAX_PER_WINDOW\";\n    31\t \"PORTAL_HANDOFF_MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES\";\n    33\t    public static final String ASSERTION_ATTRIBUTE_ENV = \"PORTAL_ASSERTION_ATTRIBUTE\";\n    34\t\n    35\t    public static final int SessionHandoffSettings fromEnvironment() {\n    64\t        return load(new java.util.function.Function<String, String>() {\n    65\t            @Override\n    66\t            public String apply(String name) {\n    67\t                return System.getenv(name);\n    68\t            }\n    69\t        });\n    70\t    }\n    71\t\n    72\t    /**\n    73\t     * @param lookup returns the value of a named setting, or {@code null} when unset. Consequently this\n    10\t * type is written so that the identifier cannot leak through the paths that normally leak secrets:\n    11\t *\n    12\t * <ul>\n    13\t *   <li>{@link #toString()} accepted identifier. 32 base64url characters carry well over 128 bits. */\n    29\t    public static final int MIN_LENGTH = 32;\n    30\t\n    31\t    /** Longest accepted identifier, bounding the work and memory spent per request. */\n    32\t    public static final int MAX_LENGTH = 128;\n    33\t\n    34\t    /**\n    35\t     * Minimum number of distinct characters in an identifier <b>that this code minted</b>.\n    36\t     *\n    37\t     * <p>Deliberately <i>not</i> applied to identifiers arriving from the trusted session owner, and\n    38\t     * the reason is a measurement: an \"at least 12 distinct symbols\" rule applied to a 32-character\n    39\t     * lowercase-hex identifier refuses <b>1.70%</b> of uniformly random ones (5 772 905 916 802 455\n    40\t     * 845 865 331 391 890 579 456 of 16<sup>32</sup>; about one sign-in in 59), because a 16-symbol\n    41\t     * alphabet simply does not offer many distinct symbols in 32 positions. Hex is what several\n    42\t     * widely deployed containers hand out, so as an input rule this heuristic was not a guard at all\n    43\t     * -- it was a random failure injected into somebody else's working system, and every one of\n    44\t     * those refusals landed on a value the authoritative store had already vouched for.\n    45\t     *\n    46\t     * <p>It also asks a question this component has no standing to ask. Rule 1 asks for type,\n    47\t     * length, character set and range; \"how many distinct symbols did the issuer's generator happen\n    48\t     * to emit\" is none of those, and the entropy of a session identifier is the property of whoever\n    49\t     * generates it, which here is the trusted owner of the session state. A degenerate value either\n    50\t     * is in the store -- in which case the owner created it and refusing it punishes their user -- or\n    51\t     * it is not, in which case the lookup refuses it on the evidence that matters.\n    52\t     *\n    53\t     * <p>For values this class produces the check is worth keeping, because the only plausible cause\n    54\t     * is a regression in the CSPRNG or the encoder below: the false-rejection chance for a\n    55\t     * 43-character base64url encoding of 32 random bytes is 8.0e-22, so it can never lock out a real\n    56\t     * user, while it does catch an RNG that has started returning constants.\n    57\t     */\n    58\t    static final int SELF_TEST_MIN_DISTINCT_CHARS = 12;\n    59\t\n    60\t    /** Hex characters of the SHA-256 fingerprint that is safe to put in a log line. */\n    61\t    private static final int FINGERPRINT_HEX = 12;\n    62\t\n    63\t    /** Domain-separation prefix so a fingerprint cannot be matched against another system's digest. */\n    64\t    private static final String FINGERPRINT_PREFIX = \"acme-sid-v1:\";\n    65\t\n    66\t    private final String value;\n    67\t\n    68\t    private SessionId(String value) {\n    69\t        this.value = value;\n    70\t    }\n    71\t\n    72\t    /** Why a candidate identifier was refused. Names are safe to log; the value is not. */\n    73\t    enum Rejection {\n    74\t        /** No identifier was supplied at all. */\n    75\t        NONE,\n    76\t        ABSENT,\n    77\t        TOO_SHORT,\n    78\t        TOO_LONG,\n    79\t        ILLEGAL_CHARACTER,\n    80\t        LOW_CHAR_DIVERSITY\n    81\t    }\n    82\t\n    83\t    /**\n    84\t     * Accepts an identifier that came from the trusted session owner's server-side state.\n    85\t     *\n    86\t     * @throws InvalidSessionIdentifierException if the value is not a well-formed opaque token.\n    87\t     *     The exception carries only the reason class, never any part of the value.\n    88\t     */\n    89\t    public static SessionId fromTrustedComponent(String raw) throws InvalidSessionIdentifierException {\n    90\t        Rejection rejection = inspect(raw);\n    91\t        if (rejection != Rejection.NONE) {\n    92\t            throw new InvalidSessionIdentifierException(rejection);\n    93\t        }\n    94\t        return new SessionId(raw);\n    95\t    }\n    96\t\n    97\t    /**\n    98\t     * Classifies a candidate without constructing an instance, so the caller can log the reason\n    99\t     * without ever handling the value as a validated secret.\n   100\t     *\n   101\t     * <p>Exactly three rules, all of them rule 1's dimensions and all of them decidable without\n   102\t     * knowing the issuer: it is text, of the right length, made of the right characters. Nothing here\n   103\t     * guesses at entropy or \"looks random\", because a value's entropy belongs to whoever issued it\n   104\t     * and this component is not the judge of that (see\n   105\t     * {@link #SELF_TEST_MIN_DISTINCT_CHARS} for the measurement that removed such a rule from this\n   106\t     * path).\n   107\t     *\n   108\t     * <p>Order matters: the null and length checks bound the character scan, and no check is\n   109\t     * preceded by trimming or case folding, so a trailing control character is refused instead of\n   110\t     * silently deleted (which would fold two inputs onto one session).\n   111\t     */\n   112\t    static Rejection inspect(String raw) {\n   113\t        if (raw == null) {\n   114\t            return Rejection.ABSENT;\n   115\t        }\n   116\t        int length = raw.length();\n   117\t        if (length < MIN_LENGTH) {\n   118\t            return Rejection.TOO_SHORT;\n   119\t        }\n   120\t        if (length > MAX_LENGTH) {\n   121\t            return Rejection.TOO_LONG;\n   122\t        }\n   123\t        for (int i = 0; Only ever asked about this class's own output.\n   154\t     */\n   155\t    static int distinctCharacters(String value) {\n   156\t        boolean[] seen = new boolean[128];\n   157\t        int distinct = 0;\n   158\t        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n   159\t            char c = value.charAt(i);\n   160\t            // The table size is tied to the allowlist, so a charset widened past it must not turn\n   161\t            // into an ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException. This\n   176\t     * single allowlist is what makes it impossible for the identifier to terminate the cookie\n   177\t     * value, inject a cookie attribute ({@code ; Domain=...}), inject a second {@code Set-Cookie}\n   178\t     * header, or break out of a log line. This is the public API for whoever <i>is</i> the session owner -- the trusted component\n   195\t     * that creates sessions -- and rule 4's preferred answer for it, because it is the same 32 bytes\n   196\t     * from the platform CSPRNG encoded into a value this package's validator accepts, rather than a\n   197\t     * hand-rolled token generator somewhere else. Deliberately package private. */\n   223\t    String value() {\n   224\t        return value;\n   225\t    }\n   226\t\n   227\t    /**\n   228\t     * A truncated SHA-256 fingerprint of the identifier, safe to log <i>for identifiers with real\n   229\t     * entropy</i> -- see the caveat, because \"safe to log\" is a claim about the input too.\n   230\t     *\n   231\t     * <p>48 bits of digest over a 256-bit input cannot be inverted or replayed; it lets an operator\n   232\t     * correlate the log lines that belong to one session. Against a full-entropy identifier an\n   233\t     * attacker who steals the logs learns nothing: guessing a candidate and checking it against 48\n   234\t     * bits of digest costs 2<sup>48</sup> tries per line and yields no usable value.\n   235\t     *\n   236\t     * <p><b>The caveat, stated as a deployment requirement.</b> A digest is a verifier, not just a\n   237\t     * mask. If the session owner hands this component identifiers drawn from a space smaller than\n   238\t     * the 48 bits it publishes -- a short counter, a truncated UUID, a timestamp with a fixed prefix\n   239\t     * -- then the log line becomes an offline test oracle for that small space, and anyone holding\n   240\t     * the logs can confirm guesses without touching the server. The requirement on the integration is\n   241\t     * therefore at least 128 bits of unpredictable entropy in every identifier it asserts (the same\n   242\t     * bar {@link #generate()} meets), and this component cannot enforce it without doing exactly the\n   243\t     * guessing-about-entropy it just refused to do in {@link #inspect}. It needs one boolean from whoever owns that state, and a compile-time dependency on a\n    13\t * datastore would either drag a driver in or force a second HTTP hop with its own timeout, retry and\n    14\t * failure semantics to get right.\n    15\t *\n    16\t * <p>The two risks a loader introduces are both closed here:\n    17\t * <ul>\n    18\t *   <li><b>Ambiguity.</b> More than one provider is a configuration error with an obvious\n    19\t *       security consequence -- which store wins? -- so it is refused rather than resolved by\n    20\t *       classpath order.</li>\n    21\t *   <li><b>Foreign code.</b> Whatever is loaded is the authority on \"is this session valid\", so its\n    22\t *       code location is handed to {@link DeploymentHygiene} and a writable location stops the\n    23\t *       component from serving at all. The discovery below is deliberately shaped so that check\n    24\t *       happens <i>before</i> the candidate is instantiated: a hygiene verdict reached after the\n    25\t *       class has run its static initialiser and constructor reports a compromise, it does not\n    26\t *       prevent one.</li>\n    27\t * </ul>\n    28\t *\n    29\t * <p>The interface is declared in this package and implemented by the application; {@code\n    30\t * META-INF/services} names the implementation. They must not log the value, and they must never fall back to reading a cookie, a query\n    16\t * parameter, a header, or a body field: those are client-supplied and would turn this component into\n    17\t * a session-fixing oracle.\n    18\t */\n    19\tpublic interface TrustedSessionAssertionSource {\n    20\t\n    21\t    /**\n    22\t     * @return the asserted session identifier, or {@code null} when the authenticator asserted none\n    23\t     */\n    24\t    String assertedSessionId(HttpServletRequest request);\n    25\t}\nFILE src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/WorkEqualizer.java\n     1\tpackage com.acme.portal.session;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * Does a fixed, small amount of throwaway computation on every request.\n     5\t *\n     6\t * <p><b>What it equalizes.</b> A refusal that happens <i>before</i> the session-store lookup is\n     7\t * measurably cheaper than one that happens after it. The response body already hides which check\n     8\t * refused, but latency does not: an attacker who can time the endpoint can tell \"this candidate is\n     9\t * not even well-formed\" from \"this candidate is well-formed but unknown\", which is a per-candidate\n    10\t * signal for anyone generating identifiers. Padding every outcome to the cost of the most expensive\n    11\t * one removes that signal.\n    12\t *\n    13\t * <p>The work is a fixed number of SHA-256 compressions over a constant buffer: no secret, no\n    14\t * attacker-controlled length, no I/O, and a cost independent of every input, which is what makes it\n    15\t * a level playing field rather than a new side channel.\n    16\t *\n    17\t * <p><b>Why it runs before every gate, including the throttle.</b> A cheap path is itself a signal,\n    18\t * so the padding is applied first and unconditionally. That costs something real, and it is a trade\n    19\t * rather than a free win: a request the rate limiter refuses still pays for the padding, so the\n    20\t * limiter sheds the session-store round trip and the cookie write, not this. Moving the padding off\n    21\t * the limited path would make \"you are being throttled for this address\" readable as a latency\n    22\t * difference, and would leave the availability refusal as a cheap probe for whether this deployment\n    23\t * is wired up at all. The accepted trade is therefore explicit -- constant work on every path, and a\n    24\t * constant small enough that this is a capacity cost of tens of microseconds rather than the lever a\n    25\t * flooding attacker would rather press.\n    26\t *\n    27\t * <p>The count was measured on this machine, not guessed: see the harness {@code TIMING} section,\n    28\t * which prints per-outcome microsecond timings and asserts both an absolute floor and a spread bound.\n    29\t * A padding constant that is never measured is how a guard like this silently stops working: if the\n    30\t * padding is smaller than the difference it is meant to hide, deleting it changes nothing observable\n    31\t * and no test can notice.\n    32\t */\n    33\tfinal class WorkEqualizer {\n    34\t\n    35\t    /**\n    36\t     * SHA-256 compressions per request. Cost here is about {@code 3 us + 0.35 us x n}, so 160 lands\n    37\t     * near 60 us -- but the harness measures the call in the same run that checks it, so neither\n    38\t     * figure is taken on trust.\n    39\t     *\n    40\t     * <p>The sizing is two-sided, and the quantity to size against is the spread between whole request\n    41\t     * paths, not the cost of the decision inside them. Measured in the harness: the cheapest path (the\n    42\t     * availability refusal -- padding plus a 503) and the dearest (a refusal that also throttles, calls\n    43\t     * the store and writes the body) differ by roughly 24 us. A padding of {@code P} therefore leaves a\n    44\t     * spread of {@code (P + 24) / P}: staying inside 2x needs {@code P >= 24 us}, and a margin over\n    45\t     * that needs about twice it. That is what 160 buys, and it measured 1.45x.\n    46\t     *\n    47\t     * <p>An earlier value of 48 was sized against the wrong quantity -- the 3.0-4.5 us delta\n    48\t     * <i>inside</i> the decision -- and the harness reported it: padding measured 18 us, the spread\n    49\t     * came out 2.59x, and the availability refusal answered in 17 us against 41-44 us for everything\n    50\t     * else. That is precisely the cheap-and-distinguishable path this class exists to remove, which is\n    51\t     * worth writing down because \"the padding is too big\" and \"the padding is too small\" are the same\n    52\t     * mistake made in opposite directions. At the other extreme, 200 compressions (~79 us) was three\n    53\t     * times the work it hid and was charged to requests the throttle had already refused, so the\n    54\t     * component spent more CPU declining than the session store spent answering.\n    55\t     *\n    56\t     * <p>The upper end is why the answer is not simply a much larger number. The padding is charged\n    57\t     * before every gate, so 60 us caps one core at roughly 16 000 requests per second doing nothing\n    58\t     * else -- the deliberate price of an answer whose timing says nothing, and still less than the\n    59\t     * store round trip that sits behind the throttle. The harness asserts both ends against values it\n    60\t     * measures in its own run: that the padding is real work, and that it has not grown back into a\n    61\t     * lever.\n    62\t     *\n    63\t     * <p>What it cannot assert is a particular deployment's throughput budget; if a site needs the last\n    64\t     * of those microseconds, the honest options are a cheaper decision path or a written acceptance of\n    65\t     * a weaker timing property, not an unmeasured constant.\n    66\t     */\n    67\t    private static final int COMPRESSIONS = 160;\n    68\t\n    69\t    private static final ThreadLocal<java.security.MessageDigest> SHA256 =\n    70\t            new ThreadLocal<java.security.MessageDigest>() {\n    71\t                @Override\n    72\t                protected java.security.MessageDigest initialValue() {\n    73\t                    try {\n    74\t                        return java.security.MessageDigest.getInstance(\"SHA-256\");\n    75\t                    } catch (java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException impossible) {\n    76\t                        throw new IllegalStateException(\"SHA-256 unavailable\", impossible);\n    77\t                    }\n    78\t                }\n    79\t            };\n    80\t\n    81\t    /**\n    82\t     * Constant input: the digest value is discarded and its length never varies. It is never the\n    83\t     * loop's working buffer -- {@link #apply} clones it -- because the loop zeroes whatever it ends\n    84\t     * on, and writing zeros into this shared array would silently turn every later request's padding\n    85\t     * into a digest of nothing. That is invisible while the loop runs and total the moment someone\n    86\t     * sets {@link #COMPRESSIONS} to zero, which is exactly the kind of defect a shared mutable array\n    87\t     * should not be allowed to hide.\n    88\t     */\n    89\t    private static final byte[] CONSTANT_INPUT = \"acme-work-equalizer-constant-input\".getBytes(\n    90\t            java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.US_ASCII);\n    91\t\n    92\t    private WorkEqualizer() {\n    93\t    }\n    94\t\n    95\t    static void apply() {\n    96\t        java.security.MessageDigest digest = SHA256.get();\n    97\t        byte[] carry = CONSTANT_INPUT.clone();\n    98\t        for (int i = 0; i < COMPRESSIONS; i++) {\n    99\t            digest.reset();\n   100\t            digest.update(carry);\n   101\t            carry = digest.digest();\n   102\t        }\n   103\t        // Keep the result reachable so the loop cannot be treated as dead code, and zero the private\n   104\t        // copy so the intermediate digest does not outlive the call.\n   105\t        java.util.Arrays.fill(carry, (byte) 0);\n   106\t    }\n   107\t\n   108\t    /** Exposed for the harness, which tunes {@link #COMPRESSIONS} against measured cost. */\n   109\t    static int compressions() {\n   110\t        return COMPRESSIONS;\n   111\t    }\n   112\t}\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

No exception is thrown: a malformed address must degrade to a\n   187\t     *         shared limited bucket, never to an unlimited one, and never to a stack trace.\n   188\t     */\n   189\t    public static ClientAddress parse(String remoteAddr) {\n   190\t        if (remoteAddr == null || remoteAddr.isEmpty() || remoteAddr.length() > MAX_RAW_LENGTH) {\n   191\t            return UNKNOWN;\n   192\t        }\n   193\t        // Reject structural surprises before the parser sees them, so no bracket, scope-id,\n   194\t        // percent-encoding or control character can reach InetAddress parsing. This loop is redundant\n   195\t        // with the two grammars below -- they admit the same five classes of character and nothing else\n   196\t        // -- and it is kept as the cheap gate that says so in one line. The harness sweeps every\n   197\t        // character this refuses against both grammars, so if either grammar is ever loosened the\n   198\t        // redundancy ends in a failing test rather than in a resolver round trip per request.\n   199\t        for (int i = 0; i < remoteAddr.length(); i++) {\n   200\t            char c = remoteAddr.charAt(i);\n   201\t            boolean allowed = (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'F')\n   202\t                    || c == '.' || c == ':';\n   203\t            if (!allowed) {\n   204\t                return UNKNOWN;\n   205\t            }\n   206\t        }\n   207\t        boolean ipv6 = remoteAddr.indexOf(':') >= 0;\n   208\t        if (ipv6) {\n   209\t            if (remoteAddr.length() > MAX_IPV6_LENGTH || !isIpv6Literal(remoteAddr)) {\n   210\t                return UNKNOWN;\n   211\t            }\n   212\t        } else if (!isDottedQuad(remoteAddr)) {\n   213\t            return UNKNOWN;\n   214\t        }\n   215\t        InetAddress address;\n   216\t        try {\n   217\t            // Only two shapes can arrive here -- a four-group dotted quad and a complete IPv6\n   218\t            // literal -- and both are literals, so getByName takes its parse-without-a-lookup path\n   219\t            // and the resolver is never consulted. That guarantee is what the two shape checks exist\n   220\t            // to provide: a DNS round trip per request would turn the limiter into an amplifier\n   221\t            // driven by an attacker-supplied address, and reverse lookups are never requested either.\n   222\t            address = InetAddress.getByName(remoteAddr);\n   223\t        } catch (IllegalArgumentException malformed) {\n   224\t            return UNKNOWN;\n   225\t        } catch (UnknownHostException notAnAddress) {\n   226\t            return UNKNOWN;\n   227\t        }\n   228\t        byte[] bytes = address.getAddress();\n   229\t        if (address instanceof java.net.Inet4Address) {\n   230\t            return new ClientAddress(address.getHostAddress(), 32, false);\n   231\t        }\n   232\t        if (address instanceof java.net.Inet6Address) {\n   233\t            // A dual-stack container reports an IPv4 peer as ::ffff:a.b.c.d. That is the same host as\n   234\t            // a.b.c.d and must not get a second lane, so the mapped form folds onto the IPv4 bucket.\n   235\t            // The deprecated IPv4-*compatible* form (::a.b.c.d, obsoleted by RFC 4291) is deliberately\n   236\t            // not folded any more: folding it is what used to turn \"::1\" into the bucket key\n   237\t            // \"0.0.0.1/32\" -- an IPv6 loopback peer asserting an IPv4 identity, merged with an address\n   238\t            // that never appears on a wire. Nothing a container actually emits needs that fold, so an\n   239\t            // unroutable legacy spelling now falls into the plain /64 lane like any other v6 peer.\n   240\t            if (isIpv4Mapped(bytes)) {\n   241\t                return new ClientAddress(\n   242\t                        (bytes[12] & 0xFF) + \".\" + (bytes[13] & 0xFF) + \".\"\n   243\t                                + (bytes[14] & 0xFF) + \".\" + (bytes[15] & 0xFF),\n   244\t                        32, false);\n   245\t            }\n   246\t            // /64 is the allocation unit an attacker commonly controls wholesale (and the unit a\n   247\t            // /64-routing site hands out), so limiting per address would be a joke while limiting\n   248\t            // per /64 stays fair to a normal dual-stack client. One consequence is worth writing down\n   249\t            // instead of leaving to chance: every address whose top 64 bits are zero -- \"::1\" first --\n   250\t            // shares the single lane \"0:0:0:0/64\". Those addresses are not reachable over a public\n   251\t            // link, so that lane holds local processes and port-forwarded sockets, and merging them\n   252\t            // is the conservative direction: it can delay a local caller, it cannot hand anyone a\n   253\t            // wider lane than their own address would have had.\n   254\t            return new ClientAddress(canonicalIpv6Prefix(bytes), 64, false);\n   255\t        }\n   256\t        return UNKNOWN;\n   257\t    }\n   258\t\n   259\t    /** True for {@code ::ffff:a.b.c.d}, the form a dual-stack socket reports an IPv4 peer as. */\n   260\t    private static boolean isIpv4Mapped(byte[] bytes) {\n   261\t        if (bytes.length != 16) {\n   262\t            return false;\n   263\t        }\n   264\t        for (int i = 0; i < 10; i++) {\n   265\t            if (bytes[i] != 0) {\n   266\t                return false;\n   267\t            }\n   268\t        }\n   269\t        return bytes[10] == (byte) 0xFF && bytes[11] == (byte) 0xFF;\n   270\t    }\n   271\t\n   272\t    /** Lower-case canonical text of the top 64 bits, e.g. {@code 2001:db8:0:1}. */\n   273\t    private static String canonicalIpv6Prefix(byte[] bytes) {\n   274\t        StringBuilder text = new StringBuilder(19);\n   275\t        for (int group = 0; group < 4; group++) {\n   276\t            if (group > 0) {\n   277\t                text.append(':');\n   278\t            }\n   279\t            int value = ((bytes[group * 2] & 0xFF) << 8) | (bytes[group * 2 + 1] & 0xFF);\n   280\t            text.append(Integer.toHexString(value));\n   281\t        }\n   282\t        return text.toString().toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT);\n   283\t    }\n   284\t\n   285\t    /** Bucket key: canonical text plus the prefix length, so v4 and v6 lanes never collide. */\n   286\t    public String key() {\n   287\t        return canonical + '/' + prefixBits;\n   288\t    }\n   289\t\n   290\t    /**\n   291\t     * Whether this bucket shares the allowance with every other unknown-source request. Callers\n   292\t     * apply a stricter ceiling to it: a request whose source cannot be established is exactly the\n   293\t     * shape an attacker would forge.\n   294\t     */\n   295\t    public boolean isUnknown() {\n   296\t        return unknown;\n   297\t    }\n   298\t\n   299\t    /** Canonical text only. It is the caller's own address, so it is not a secret. */\n   300\t    @Override\n   301\t    public String toString() {\n   302\t        return canonical;\n   303\t    }\n   304\t\n   305\t    @Override\n   306\t    public boolean equals(Object other) {\n   307\t        if (this == other) {\n   308\t            return true;\n   309\t        }\n   310\t        if (!(other instanceof ClientAddress)) {\n   311\t            return false;\n   312\t        }\n   313\t        return key().equals(((ClientAddress) other).key());\n   314\t    }\n   315\t\n   316\t    @Override\n   317\t    public int hashCode() {\n   318\t        return key().hashCode();\n   319\t    }\n   320\t}\nFILE src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/DeploymentHygiene.java\n     1\tpackage com.acme.portal.session;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.io.File;\n     4\timport java.io.IOException;\n     5\timport java.nio.file.Files;\n     6\timport java.nio.file.Path;\n     7\timport java.nio.file.Paths;\n     8\timport java.security.CodeSource;\n     9\timport java.security.ProtectionDomain;\n    10\timport java.util.LinkedHashSet;\n    11\timport java.util.Set;\n    12\t\n    13\t/**\n    14\t * Startup check for the one class of deployment mistake that turns a correct component into remote\n    15\t * code execution: loading classes from somewhere another local account can write.\n    16\t *\n    17\t * <p>The component reads no files itself, so file permissions would be moot if it stayed alone. But\n    18\t * it does one thing that pulls the environment in: it discovers the application's session verifier\n    19\t * through a service loader, and a loader follows the classpath. If any location on that path is\n    20\t * writable by another account, that account can supply the code that answers \"is this session\n    21\t * valid\", and every guard in this package becomes decoration. Rule 8 asks for exactly this check, so\n    22\t * it is performed and enforced rather than assumed.\n    23\t *\n    24\t * <p>What is inspected: every JVM classpath entry, plus the code-source location (and its parent\n    25\t * directory, because a writable directory lets a file be replaced even when the file is not) of this\n    26\t * class and of the resolved verifier -- the two code bases that actually decide the outcome.\n    27\t *\n    28\t * <p>Descriptions name a path and nothing else. Paths are configuration, not secrets, and the text\n    29\t * stays terse because it reaches a log line.\n    30\t */\n    31\tfinal class DeploymentHygiene {\n    32\t\n    33\t    private static final int MAX_ENTRIES = 512;\n    34\t    private static final int MAX_PATH_LENGTH = 4096;\n    35\t\n    36\t    private DeploymentHygiene() {\n    37\t    }\n    38\t\n    39\t    /**\n    40\t     * @param codeOwners classes whose own code location must be trustworthy; typically this class and\n    41\t     *                   the resolved session verifier\n    42\t     * @return {@code null} when the deployment looks safe, otherwise a one-line reason to refuse\n    43\t     *         service. An unreadable location is reported as a problem: \"unknown\" is not \"safe\".\n    44\t     */\n    45\t    static String describeProblem(Class<?>... codeOwners) {\n    46\t        Set<Path> locations = new LinkedHashSet<Path>();\n    47\t        String classpath = System.getProperty(\"java.class.path\", \"\");\n    48\t        if (!classpath.isEmpty()) {\n    49\t            String[] entries = classpath.split(File.pathSeparator);\n    50\t            if (entries.length > MAX_ENTRIES) {\n    51\t                return \"the JVM classpath has more than \" + MAX_ENTRIES + \" entries\";\n    52\t            }\n    53\t            for (String entry : entries) {\n    54\t                Path path = toPath(entry);\n    55\t                if (path != null) {\n    56\t                    locations.add(path);\n    57\t                }\n    58\t            }\n    59\t        }\n    60\t        for (Class<?> codeOwner : codeOwners) {\n    61\t            if (codeOwner == null) {\n    62\t                return \"a required code owner was not supplied\";\n    63\t            }\n    64\t            Path fromCodeSource = codeSourceLocation(codeOwner);\n    65\t            if (fromCodeSource == null) {\n    66\t                // Cannot establish where this class was loaded from, so cannot prove it is not\n    67\t                // somebody else's to write. Unknown is not safe.\n    68\t                return \"the code location of \" + codeOwner.getName() + \" could not be established\";\n    69\t            }\n    70\t            locations.add(fromCodeSource);\n    71\t        }\n    72\t        for (Path location : locations) {\n    73\t            if (!Files.exists(location)) {\n    74\t                // A classpath entry with nothing there is not evidence of safety: the verdict was\n    75\t                // reached without seeing a single permission bit, and an entry that was removed (or\n    76\t                // was never extracted) between the packaging step and this check is precisely the\n    77\t                // window in which a location changes hands. Reported, not skipped.\n    78\t                return \"a code location named by the deployment is not present to be inspected: \"\n    79\t                        + logSafe(location);\n    80\t            }\n    81\t            if (worldWritable(location)) {\n    82\t                return \"code is loadable from a location other users can write: \" + logSafe(location);\n    83\t            }\n    84\t            Path parent = location.getParent();\n    85\t            if (parent != null && Files.isDirectory(parent) && worldWritable(parent)) {\n    86\t                return \"code is loadable from a directory other users can write: \" + logSafe(parent);\n    87\t            }\n    88\t        }\n    89\t        return null;\n    90\t    }\n    91\t\n    92\t    /**\n    93\t     * Text for a refusal reason, which ends up on the console.\n    94\t     *\n    95\t     * <p>A path is configuration, not a secret -- but it is also the one string here that a packaging\n    96\t     * step, and therefore anything that can write a build, influences. Control characters stripped\n    97\t     * rather than escaped, because a filename carrying CR or LF is the only way this component could\n    98\t     * ever be made to write a log line it did not author. Everything else, including every other byte\n    99\t     * of the path, is passed through so the operator still recognises it.\n   100\t     */\n   101\t    private static String logSafe(Path location) {\n   102\t        String text = String.valueOf(location);\n   103\t        StringBuilder safe = new StringBuilder(Math.min(text.length(), MAX_PATH_LENGTH));\n   104\t        for (int i = 0; i < text.length() && safe.length() < MAX_PATH_LENGTH; i++) {\n   105\t            char c = text.charAt(i);\n   106\t            if (c >= 0x20 && c != 0x7F) {\n   107\t                safe.append(c);\n   108\t            }\n   109\t        }\n   110\t        return safe.length() == 0 ? \"(unnamed location)\" : safe.toString();\n   111\t    }\n   112\t\n   113\t    private static Path toPath(String entry) {\n   114\t        if (entry == null || entry.isEmpty() || entry.length() > MAX_PATH_LENGTH) {\n   115\t            return null;\n   116\t        }\n   117\t        try {\n   118\t            return Paths.get(entry);\n   119\t        } catch (RuntimeException | Error unparseable) {\n   120\t            return null;\n   121\t        }\n   122\t    }\n   123\t\n   124\t    private static Path codeSourceLocation(Class<?> owner) {\n   125\t        try {\n   126\t            ProtectionDomain domain = owner.getProtectionDomain();\n   127\t            CodeSource source = domain == null ? null : domain.getCodeSource();\n   128\t            if (source == null || source.getLocation() == null) {\n   129\t                return null;\n   130\t            }\n   131\t            java.net.URL location = source.getLocation();\n   132\t            if (!\"file\".equalsIgnoreCase(location.getProtocol())) {\n   133\t                // A jar over http or a nested module location cannot be judged by POSIX bits, and\n   134\t                // neither is a directory an attacker could have written in place.\n   135\t                return null;\n   136\t            }\n   137\t            return toPath(java.net.URLDecoder.decode(location.getPath(),\n   138\t                    java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n   139\t        } catch (RuntimeException | Error unavailable) {\n   140\t            return null;\n   141\t        }\n   142\t    }\n   143\t\n   144\t    private static boolean worldWritable(Path path) {\n   145\t        try {\n   146\t            Set<java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission> permissions =\n   147\t                    Files.getPosixFilePermissions(path);\n   148\t            return permissions.contains(java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE);\n   149\t        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException notPosix) {\n   150\t            // A filesystem without POSIX bits cannot be judged here. Fail closed: the integrator has\n   151\t            // to run this component where the check can actually be made.\n   152\t            return true;\n   153\t        } catch (IOException unreadable) {\n   154\t            return true;\n   155\t        }\n   156\t    }\n   157\t}\nFILE src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/ResponseBody.java\n     1\tpackage com.acme.portal.session;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * The only two bodies this component ever writes.\n     5\t *\n     6\t * <p>Both are constant. The refusal body in particular is a compile-time constant shared by every\n     7\t * refusal path, so a response can never disclose <i>why</i> it refused: not which check failed, not\n     8\t * whether an identifier exists, not whether the component is misconfigured. That is rule 6's\n     9\t * \"identical messages\" requirement, made structural rather than a convention a maintainer has to\n    10\t * remember.\n    11\t *\n    12\t * <p>Neither body contains the session identifier, a client address, a stack trace, a class name or a\n    13\t * value read from the environment. Both are pure ASCII, built without any control character appearing\n    14\t * in a source literal.\n    15\t */\n    16\tfinal class ResponseBody {\n    17\t\n    18\t    /** Byte-identical for every refusal, whatever the reason. */\n    19\t    static final String REFUSAL = \"<!DOCTYPE html>\"\n    20\t            + \"<html lang=\\\"en\\\"><head><meta charset=\\\"utf-8\\\">\"\n    21\t            + \"<title>Sign-in</title></head><body>\"\n    22\t            + \"<h1>Sign-in</h1>\"\n    23\t            + \"<p>We could not complete sign-in. Please start again.</p>\"\n    24\t            + \"</body></html>\";\n    25\t\n    26\t    /**\n    27\t     * Bound on the escaped <b>output</b>, so a long configured value cannot inflate a page.\n    28\t     *\n    29\t     * <p>The distinction matters, and this constant used to get it wrong by bounding the input\n    30\t     * instead: escaping only ever grows, so 256 characters of {@code \"} are 1 536 bytes of\n    31\t     * {@code &quot;} -- six times the limit, on a page whose guard had just reported itself satisfied.\n    32\t     * The loop therefore checks the room a character <i>needs</i> before emitting it, which keeps the\n    33\t     * result inside the bound and also guarantees it never cuts an entity in half: {@code &am} or\n    34\t     * {@code &quo} left in a document is broken markup, worse than a page that stops one entity early.\n    35\t     */\n    36\t    private static final int MAX_ESCAPED_LENGTH = 256;\n    37\t\n    38\t    /** Widest replacement this escaper can emit for a single input character ({@code &quot;}). */\n    39\t    private static final int WIDEST_ENTITY = 6;\n    40\t\n    41\t    /** Substitute for a control character; a space, never an NUL. */\n    42\t    private static final char SUBSTITUTE = (char) 0x20;\n    43\t\n    44\t    private ResponseBody() {\n    45\t    }\n    46\t\n    47\t    /**\n    48\t     * Success page. Your session is held in the cookie <code>\"\n    58\t                + escapeHtml(policy.name()) + \"</code>.</p>\"\n    59\t                + \"</body></html>\";\n    60\t    }\n    61\t\n    62\t    /**\n    63\t     * Contextual escaping for HTML text. Both inputs come from validated configuration rather than\n    64\t     * from a client, and are escaped anyway: output encoding is cheap and the alternative is\n    65\t     * depending on a validator never regressing.\n    66\t     */\n    67\t    static String escapeHtml(String value) {\n    68\t        if (value == null) {\n    69\t            return \"\";\n    70\t        }\n    71\t        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(Math.min(MAX_ESCAPED_LENGTH, value.length() + 16));\n    72\t        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n    73\t            char c = value.charAt(i);\n    74\t            String replacement;\n    75\t            switch (c) {\n    76\t                case '&':\n    77\t                    replacement = \"&amp;\";\n    78\t                    break;\n    79\t                case '<':\n    80\t                    replacement = \"&lt;\";\n    81\t                    break;\n    82\t                case '>':\n    83\t                    replacement = \"&gt;\";\n    84\t                    break;\n    85\t                case '\"':\n    86\t                    replacement = \"&quot;\";\n    87\t                    break;\n    88\t                case '\\'':\n    89\t                    replacement = \"&#39;\";\n    90\t                    break;\n    91\t                default:\n    92\t                    if (c < 0x20 || c == 0x7F) {\n    93\t                        // Never emitted, even as an entity: a control byte in a page is a bug worth\n    94\t                        // seeing as a marker, not a character worth rendering.\n    95\t                        replacement = String.valueOf(SUBSTITUTE);\n    96\t                    } else {\n    97\t                        replacement = String.valueOf(c);\n    98\t                    }\n    99\t            }\n   100\t            // Charged against the widest replacement this escaper can emit, so the emitted length can\n   101\t            // never pass the bound however the characters fall -- and an entity is always whole or\n   102\t            // absent. A malformed or unrecognised setting raises\n    20\t * {@link IllegalArgumentException} and the component refuses to start: there is no permissive\n    21\t * default for a security-relevant attribute and no silent coercion (a typo such as\n    22\t * {@code SameSide=Strict} fails loudly at deployment instead of quietly weakening the cookie).\n    23\t * Only a genuinely absent or empty value falls back to the hardened default below.\n    24\t */\n    25\tpublic final class SessionCookiePolicy {\n    26\t\n    27\t    /**\n    28\t     * Default name uses the {@code __Host-} cookie prefix, which tells a conforming browser to\n    29\t     * discard the cookie unless it is Secure, host-only and {@code Path=/}. Both inputs come from validated configuration rather than\n    64\t     * from a client, and are escaped anyway: output encoding is cheap and the alternative is\n    65\t     * depending on a validator never regressing.\n    66\t     */\n    67\t    static String escapeHtml(String value) {\n    68\t        if (value == null) {\n    69\t            return \"\";\n    70\t        }\n    71\t        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(Math.min(MAX_ESCAPED_LENGTH, value.length() + 16));\n    72\t        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n    73\t            char c = value.charAt(i);\n    74\t            String replacement;\n    75\t            switch (c) {\n    76\t                case '&':\n    77\t                    replacement = \"&amp;\";\n    78\t                    break;\n    79\t                case '<':\n    80\t                    replacement = \"&lt;\";\n    81\t                    break;\n    82\t                case '>':\n    83\t                    replacement = \"&gt;\";\n    84\t                    break;\n    85\t                case '\"':\n    86\t                    replacement = \"&quot;\";\n    87\t                    break;\n    88\t                case '\\'':\n    89\t                    replacement = \"&#39;\";\n    90\t                    break;\n    91\t                default:\n    92\t                    if (c < 0x20 || c == 0x7F) {\n    93\t                        // Never emitted, even as an entity: a control byte in a page is a bug worth\n    94\t                        // seeing as a marker, not a character worth rendering.\n    95\t                        replacement = String.valueOf(SUBSTITUTE);\n    96\t                    } else {\n    97\t                        replacement = String.valueOf(c);\n    98\t                    }\n    99\t            }\n   100\t            // Charged against the widest replacement this escaper can emit, so the emitted length can\n   101\t            // never pass the bound however the characters fall -- and an entity is always whole or\n   102\t            // absent. Sizing the cut on the input length is what let a quote-heavy value exceed the\n   103\t            // bound sixfold, and cutting on the output length is what would leave \"&quo\" in a page.\n   104\t            if (out.length() + WIDEST_ENTITY > MAX_ESCAPED_LENGTH) {\n   105\t                break;\n   106\t            }\n   107\t            out.append(replacement);\n   108\t        }\n   109\t        return out.toString();\n   110\t    }\n   111\t}\n     1\tpackage com.acme.portal.session;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * The security decision of this component, with no servlet types in it: given what the trusted\n     5\t * session owner asserted and what the client sent, may a session cookie be issued?\n     6\t *\n     7\t * <p>Deliberately free of container and I/O dependencies, so the decision can be exercised directly\n     8\t * in tests and the HTTP layer stays a thin adapter that is safe to replace.\n     9\t */\n    10\tpublic final class SessionHandoffService {\n    11\t\n    12\t    /** Result of one handoff attempt. */\n    13\t    public enum Outcome {\n    14\t        /** A session cookie may be issued;
